### Q1. JEE Main 2026 (08 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which statements are True?
A. In Hoffmann bromamide degradation, $4$ moles of NaOH and $2$ moles of Br$_2$ are consumed per mole of an amide
B. Hoffmann bromamide reaction is not given by alkyl amides.
C. Primary amines can be synthesized by Hoffmann bromamide degradation.
D. Secondary amide on reaction with Br$_2$ and NaOH will give secondary amine.
E. The by-products of Hoffmann degradation are Na$_2$CO$_3$, NaBr and H$_2$O.
Choose the correct answer from the options given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** A, C and E only
- **(B)** B, C and D only
- **(C)** C and E only
- **(D)** C, D and E only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) C and E only**

**Detailed Solution:**

The balanced chemical equation for Hoffmann bromamide degradation is:

$\text{R-CONH}_2 + \text{Br}_2 + 4\text{NaOH} \rightarrow \text{R-NH}_2 + \text{Na}_2\text{CO}_3 + 2\text{NaBr} + 2\text{H}_2\text{O}$

Statement A is false because $1$ mole of $\text{Br}_2$ and $4$ moles of $\text{NaOH}$ are consumed per mole of amide.

Statement B is false because primary alkyl amides readily undergo this reaction.

Statement C is true as the primary product of this reaction is a primary amine.

Statement D is false because the reaction is strictly given by primary amides ($\text{R-CONH}_2$), not secondary amides.

Statement E is true as the by-products formed are $\text{Na}_2\text{CO}_3$, $\text{NaBr}$, and $\text{H}_2\text{O}$.

Therefore, only statements C and E are true.

Answer: C and E only

</details>

### Q2. JEE Main 2026 (06 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Consider the following organic reaction sequence. Choose the final product $(X)$ from the following (consider the major product in all intermediate reactions)
<img style="width: 400px" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_april_watermarked/06APRS2__q67_d1.jpg" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** Benzene
- **(B)** Phenol
- **(C)** Propanol
- **(D)** Chlorobenzene

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Benzene**

**Detailed Solution:**

The starting material is propanoic acid ($CH_3CH_2COOH$).

In step (i), propanoic acid reacts with $NH_3$ upon heating to form propanamide.
$CH_3CH_2COOH \xrightarrow{NH_3, \Delta} CH_3CH_2CONH_2$

In step (ii), propanamide undergoes Hoffmann bromamide degradation with $NaOH$ and $Br_2$ to form ethylamine.
$CH_3CH_2CONH_2 \xrightarrow{NaOH, Br_2} CH_3CH_2NH_2$

In step (iii), ethylamine reacts with nitrous acid ($HNO_2$) to form an unstable diazonium salt, which hydrolyzes to give ethanol.
$CH_3CH_2NH_2 \xrightarrow{HNO_2, H_2O} CH_3CH_2OH$

In step (iv), ethanol reacts with benzenediazonium chloride ($C_6H_5N_2Cl$). Ethanol reduces the diazonium salt to benzene, while getting oxidized to ethanal.
$CH_3CH_2OH + C_6H_5N_2Cl \rightarrow C_6H_6 + CH_3CHO + N_2 + HCl$

The final major product (X) is benzene.

Answer: Benzene

</details>

### Q3. JEE Main 2026 (06 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Consider the following sequence of reactions.
<img style="width: 600px" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_april_watermarked/06APRS1__q72_d1.png" />
The percentage of nitrogen in the yellow product (X) formed is _______%. (Nearest Integer) (Given Molar mass in g mol$^{-1}$ H:1, C:12, N:14)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **21**

**Detailed Solution:**

The given starting material is diazoaminobenzene ($C_6H_5-N=N-NH-C_6H_5$).

When diazoaminobenzene is treated with an acid catalyst (HCl) and excess aniline, and warmed to $40-45^\circ \text{C}$, it undergoes a rearrangement reaction (or dissociation followed by coupling) to form $p$-aminoazobenzene.

The reaction sequence is as follows:
1. Diazoaminobenzene reacts with HCl to form benzenediazonium chloride and aniline.
2. The benzenediazonium ion then couples with the excess aniline at the para position to yield $p$-aminoazobenzene.
3. Cooling and adding acetic acid helps in precipitating the free base, $p$-aminoazobenzene, which is a yellow dye (often called Aniline Yellow).

The structure of the yellow product (X), $p$-aminoazobenzene, is $C_6H_5-N=N-C_6H_4-NH_2$.

The molecular formula of $p$-aminoazobenzene is $C_{12}H_{11}N_3$.

Let's calculate its molar mass:
Molar mass $= (12 \times 12) + (11 \times 1) + (3 \times 14)$
Molar mass $= 144 + 11 + 42 = 197 \text{ g mol}^{-1}$

The mass of nitrogen in one mole of the compound is $3 \times 14 = 42 \text{ g}$.

The percentage of nitrogen in the product is:
$\% \text{ N} = \dfrac{42}{197} \times 100 \approx 21.32\%$

Rounding to the nearest integer, we get $21$.

Answer: $21$

</details>

### Q4. JEE Main 2026 (05 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given below are two statements :
Statement I: Heating benzamide with bromine in an ethanolic solution of sodium hydroxide will give benzylamine.
Statement II: Nitration of aniline with $HNO_3/H_2SO_4$ at $288\text{ K}$ produces $m$-nitroaniline in higher amount than $o$-nitroaniline (pH adjusted).
In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false
- **(C)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false
- **(D)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Statement I is false but Statement II is true**

**Detailed Solution:**

Statement I describes the Hoffmann bromamide degradation reaction. When benzamide ($C_6H_5CONH_2$) is heated with bromine in an ethanolic solution of sodium hydroxide, it produces aniline ($C_6H_5NH_2$), not benzylamine. Thus, Statement I is false.

Statement II describes the nitration of aniline. Direct nitration of aniline with $HNO_3/H_2SO_4$ yields a mixture of $p$-nitroaniline ($51\%$), $m$-nitroaniline ($47\%$), and $o$-nitroaniline ($2\%$). The significant formation of the meta derivative is due to the protonation of aniline in the strongly acidic medium to form the anilinium ion, which is meta-directing. Since $47\% > 2\%$, $m$-nitroaniline is produced in a higher amount than $o$-nitroaniline. Thus, Statement II is true.

Answer: Statement I is false but Statement II is true

</details>

---

### Q5. JEE Main 2026 (05 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Identify the incorrect statements.
<img style="width: 600px" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_april_watermarked/05APRS1__q68_d1.png" />
Choose the correct answer from the options given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** A and D Only
- **(B)** A and C Only
- **(C)** B and C Only
- **(D)** A and B Only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) B and C Only**

**Detailed Solution:**

Statement A: In benzylamine ($C_6H_5CH_2NH_2$), the lone pair of electrons on the nitrogen atom is localized and not involved in resonance. In aniline ($C_6H_5NH_2$), the lone pair is delocalized over the benzene ring. Hence, benzylamine is a stronger base than aniline. Statement A is correct.

Statement B: Gabriel phthalimide synthesis involves the nucleophilic substitution ($S_N2$) of an alkyl halide by the phthalimide anion. Aryl halides do not undergo nucleophilic substitution easily due to the partial double bond character of the C-X bond. Therefore, primary aromatic amines (like p-methoxyaniline) cannot be synthesized by this method. Statement B is incorrect.

Statement C: The reaction of 2-phenylacetamide ($C_6H_5CH_2CONH_2$) with $Br_2$ and $NaOH$ is the Hoffmann bromamide degradation, which yields benzylamine ($C_6H_5CH_2NH_2$). Benzylamine is a primary aliphatic (aralkyl) amine, not a primary aromatic amine (where the $-NH_2$ group must be directly attached to the benzene ring). Statement C is incorrect.

Statement D: p-Nitroaniline undergoes diazotization with $NaNO_2$ and $HCl$ at $0^\circ C$ to form p-nitrobenzenediazonium chloride. Heating this aqueous solution ($\Delta$) yields p-nitrophenol. Since p-nitrophenol is acidic, it dissolves in aqueous $NaOH$ to form a soluble sodium salt. Statement D is correct.

The incorrect statements are B and C.

Answer: B and C Only

</details>

### Q6. JEE Main 2026 (04 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The strongest conjugate acid will result from:

**Options:**

- **(A)** aniline <img style="width:80px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_april_watermarked/04APRS2__q67_d1.jpg" />
- **(B)** $4$-methoxyaniline <img style="width:80px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_april_watermarked/04APRS2__q67_d2.jpg" />
- **(C)** $4$-nitroaniline <img style="width:80px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_april_watermarked/04APRS2__q67_d3.jpg" />
- **(D)** $4$-methylaniline (p-toluidine) <img style="width:80px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_april_watermarked/04APRS2__q67_d4.jpg" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $4$-nitroaniline <img style="width:80px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_april_watermarked/04APRS2__q67_d3.jpg" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

The strength of a conjugate acid is inversely proportional to the basic strength of its corresponding base. Thus, the weakest base will form the strongest conjugate acid.

The basicity of anilines depends on the availability of the lone pair on the nitrogen atom. Electron-withdrawing groups decrease basicity by withdrawing electron density, while electron-donating groups increase basicity.

In $4$-nitroaniline, the $-\text{NO}_2$ group is a strong electron-withdrawing group due to its $-R$ and $-I$ effects. This strongly delocalizes the lone pair of electrons on the nitrogen atom, making $4$-nitroaniline the weakest base among the given options.

In contrast, $-\text{OCH}_3$ ($+R$ effect) and $-\text{CH}_3$ ($+I$ and hyperconjugation) are electron-donating groups that increase basicity, making $4$-methoxyaniline and $4$-methylaniline stronger bases than aniline.

Since $4$-nitroaniline is the weakest base, its conjugate acid is the strongest.

Answer: $4$-nitroaniline <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_april_watermarked/04APRS2__q67_d3.jpg" />

</details>

### Q7. JEE Main 2026 (04 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Product $C$ of the following reaction sequence will be
<img style="width:650px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_april_watermarked/04APRS1__q67_d1.png" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** 1-Bromo-4-nitrobenzene
- **(B)** 1, 3, 5-Tribromo-2-nitrobenzene
- **(C)** 4-Bromo-1-nitrobenzene
- **(D)** 1, 3, 5-Tribromobenzene

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 1, 3, 5-Tribromo-2-nitrobenzene**

**Detailed Solution:**

Aniline on reaction with bromine water gives 2,4,6-tribromoaniline (A) as the major product due to the strong activating nature of the $-\text{NH}_2$ group.

Reaction of (A) with $\text{NaNO}_2/\text{HCl}$ at $273-278\text{ K}$ (diazotization) yields 2,4,6-tribromobenzenediazonium chloride (B).

Treatment of (B) with $\text{HBF}_4$ forms 2,4,6-tribromobenzenediazonium fluoroborate. Heating this intermediate with $\text{NaNO}_2$ and $\text{Cu}$ replaces the diazonium group with a nitro group, forming 2,4,6-tribromonitrobenzene (C).

To assign the IUPAC name, we number the ring to give the lowest locant set to the substituents. The set (1, 2, 3, 5) is lower than (1, 2, 4, 6). Therefore, the nitro group is at position 2, and the bromo groups are at positions 1, 3, and 5. The correct IUPAC name is 1,3,5-tribromo-2-nitrobenzene.

Answer: 1, 3, 5-Tribromo-2-nitrobenzene

</details>

### Q8. JEE Main 2026 (02 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Consider the following reactions sequence
<img style="width:500px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_april_watermarked/02APRS2__q75_d1.png" />
When the product (P) is subjected to Carius analysis using $AgNO_3$, $1.0$ g of the product (P) will produce _______ g of the precipitate of AgBr. (Nearest Integer)
(Given: molar mass in g mol$^{-1}$ C : 12, H : 1, O : 16, N : 14, Br : 80, Ag : 108)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **1**

**Detailed Solution:**

The given reactant is p-nitrotoluene.

Step (i): Reduction with Sn/HCl followed by $OH^-$ converts the $-NO_2$ group to an $-NH_2$ group, yielding p-toluidine.

Step (ii): Reaction with acetic anhydride, $(CH_3CO)_2O$, protects the amino group by forming N-(4-methylphenyl)acetamide.

Step (iii): Bromination with $Br_2/AlBr_3$ directs the incoming bromine to the ortho position relative to the strongly activating $-NHCOCH_3$ group (the para position is blocked by the methyl group). This yields 2-bromo-4-methylacetanilide.

Step (iv): Acid hydrolysis with $H_3O^+$ removes the acetyl group, yielding 2-bromo-4-methylaniline as the major product (P).

The molecular formula of product P (2-bromo-4-methylaniline) is $C_7H_8NBr$.
Molar mass of P = $(7 \times 12) + (8 \times 1) + 14 + 80 = 186 \text{ g mol}^{-1}$.

In Carius analysis, the bromine in the organic compound is quantitatively converted to silver bromide (AgBr).
Molar mass of AgBr = $108 + 80 = 188 \text{ g mol}^{-1}$.

Since one molecule of P contains one bromine atom, 1 mole of P produces 1 mole of AgBr.
Moles of P in $1.0 \text{ g} = \dfrac{1.0}{186} \text{ mol}$.
Moles of AgBr produced = $\dfrac{1.0}{186} \text{ mol}$.
Mass of AgBr produced = $\dfrac{1.0}{186} \times 188 \text{ g} \approx 1.01 \text{ g}$.

Rounding to the nearest integer, the mass of the AgBr precipitate is 1 g.

Answer: $1$

</details>

### Q9. JEE Main 2026 (02 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Consider the three aromatic molecules (P, Q and R) whose structures have been given below :
<img style="width:600px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_april_watermarked/02APRS1__q68_d1.png" />
The <b>correct</b> order regarding the reactivity of these compounds with $Ph-N\equiv NCl^{(-)}_{(+)}$ under optimum but slightly acidic medium is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $P > Q > R$
- **(B)** $R > P > Q$
- **(C)** $R > Q > P$
- **(D)** $P > R > Q$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $P > Q > R$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The reaction of the given aromatic compounds with benzenediazonium chloride ($Ph-N_2^+ Cl^-$) is an azo coupling reaction, which is a type of electrophilic aromatic substitution.

The electrophile, the diazonium ion ($Ph-N_2^+$), is a weak electrophile and requires a strongly activated aromatic ring to undergo coupling. The reactivity of the ring depends on its electron density, which is enhanced by the $+M$ (mesomeric) effect of the $-NMe_2$ group.

1. In molecule P (N,N-dimethylaniline), the $-NMe_2$ group is coplanar with the benzene ring. The lone pair on the nitrogen atom is fully delocalized into the ring, providing a strong $+M$ effect and making the ring highly electron-rich and reactive.

2. In molecule Q, there is one methyl group at the ortho position to the $-NMe_2$ group. This causes steric hindrance, forcing the $-NMe_2$ group slightly out of the plane of the benzene ring. This phenomenon is known as Steric Inhibition of Resonance (SIR). As a result, the delocalization of the nitrogen lone pair into the ring is reduced, decreasing the $+M$ effect and making it less reactive than P.

3. In molecule R, there are two methyl groups at the ortho positions. This causes severe steric repulsion, forcing the $-NMe_2$ group almost completely out of the plane of the ring. The $+M$ effect is minimized because the lone pair can no longer effectively overlap with the $\pi$-system of the ring. Consequently, the ring is the least activated among the three.

Therefore, the electron density on the aromatic ring and the reactivity towards electrophilic substitution follow the order: P $>$ Q $>$ R.

Answer: $P > Q > R$

</details>

### Q10. JEE Main 2026 (28 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Total number of alkali insoluble solid sulphonamides obtained by reaction of given amines with Hinsberg's reagent is $\_\_\_\_$. 
 Aniline, N-Methylaniline, Methanamine, N, N-Dimethylmethanamine, N-Methyl methanamine, Phenylmethanamine, N-propylaniline, N-phenylaniline, N, N-Dimethylaniline, Allyl amine, Isopropyl amine

**Options:**

- **(A)** 2
- **(B)** 5
- **(C)** 4
- **(D)** 8

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 4**

**Detailed Solution:**

Hinsberg's reagent (benzenesulfonyl chloride) classifies amines by their reaction products:
Primary amines (RNH₂) form alkali-soluble sulfonamides (soluble in NaOH).
Secondary amines (R₂NH) form alkali-insoluble sulfonamides (precipitate in NaOH).
Tertiary amines (R₃N) do not react.
Analyzing the given amines:
Aniline (primary), Methanamine (primary), Phenylmethanamine (primary), Allyl amine (primary), Isopropyl amine (primary) → alkali-soluble.
N,N-Dimethylmethanamine (tertiary), N,N-Dimethylaniline (tertiary) → no reaction.
N-Methylaniline (secondary), N-Methyl methanamine (secondary), N-Propylaniline (secondary), N-Phenylaniline (secondary) → alkali-insoluble.
Total alkali-insoluble sulfonamides: 4.

</details>

### Q12. JEE Main 2026 (28 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given below are two statements: 
 <b>Statement I</b>: Griss-Ilosvay test is used for the detection of nitrite ion, which involves the use of sulphanilic acid and $\alpha$-naphthylamine reagent. 
 <b>Statement II</b>: In the above test, sulphanilic acid is diazotized by the acidified nitrite ion, which on further coupling with $\alpha$-naphthylamine forms an azo-dye. 
 In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false
- **(C)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true
- **(D)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Both Statement I and Statement II are true**

**Detailed Solution:**

The Griss-Ilosvay test for nitrite ion detection involves adding sulphanilic acid and $\alpha$-naphthylamine reagent. Acidified nitrite ions diazotize sulphanilic acid, forming a diazonium salt that undergoes azo coupling with $\alpha$-naphthylamine to produce a characteristic red azo-dye. Both statements accurately describe this test and its mechanism, making both statements true.

</details>

### Q13. JEE Main 2026 (28 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Consider the following reactions giving major product. Identify the correct reaction.

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img style="width:350px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/28S1_o_59_1_v2.png">
- **(B)** <img style="width:500px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/28S1_o_59_2_v2.png">
- **(C)** <img style="width:300px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/28S1_o_59_3_v2.png">
- **(D)** <img style="width:500px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/28S1_o_59_4_v2.png">

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <img style="width:300px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/28S1_o_59_3_v2.png">**

**Detailed Solution:**

In reaction (1), Gabriel phthalimide synthesis cannot be used to prepare aniline because aryl halides do not undergo nucleophilic substitution ($S_N2$) with potassium phthalimide due to partial double bond character of the C-X bond.

In reaction (2), the reduction of isocyanide ($R-NC$) with $Sn/HCl$ yields a secondary amine ($R-NHCH_3$), not a primary amine ($R-CH_2NH_2$).

In reaction (3), propanamide reacts with $Br_2$ and $KOH$ (Hoffmann Bromamide Degradation) to give ethanamine. This is a correct reaction.

In reaction (4), the $-NHCO-$ group is attached to two rings. The ring attached to the nitrogen atom is activated (ortho/para directing) due to the lone pair on N, while the ring attached to the carbonyl group is deactivated. Therefore, nitration should occur on the ring attached to the Nitrogen atom at the para position, not on the ring attached to the Carbonyl group.

</details>

### Q14. JEE Main 2026 (24 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

A student has planned to prepare acetanilide from aniline using acetic anhydride. The student has started from 9.3 g of aniline. However, the student has managed to obtain 11 g of dry acetanilide. The % yield of this reaction is :-

**Options:**

- **(A)** 59.5 %
- **(B)** $72.5 \%$
- **(C)** $97.5 \%$
- **(D)** $81.5 \%$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $81.5 \%$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Molar mass of aniline ($C_6H_5NH_2$) = 93 g/mol.
Moles of aniline = 9.3/93 = 0.1 mol.
From the reaction, theoretical yield of acetanilide = 0.1 mol.
Molar mass of acetanilide ($C_6H_5NHCOCH_3$) = 135 g/mol.
Theoretical mass = 0.1 × 135 = 13.5 g.
Percentage yield = (11/13.5) × 100 = 81.5%

</details>

### Q15. JEE Main 2026 (24 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Given below are two statements: 
 <b>Statement I</b>: The dipole moment of R-CN is greater than R-NC and R-NC can undergo hydrolysis under acidic medium to produce 
 <img style="width:100px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/24S2_q_54_1_v2.png">. 
 <b>Statement II</b>: R-CN hydrolyses under acidic medium to produce a compound which on treatment with $\mathrm{SOCl}_{2}$, followed by the addition of $\mathrm{NH}_{3}$ gives another compound $(\mathrm{x})$. This compound $(\mathrm{x})$ on treatment with $\mathrm{NaOCl} / \mathrm{NaOH}$ gives a product, that on treatment with $\mathrm{CHCl}_{3} / \mathrm{KOH} / \Delta$ produces R-NC. 
 In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false
- **(B)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true
- **(C)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true
- **(D)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) Statement I is false but Statement II is true**

**Detailed Solution:**

Statement I: The dipole moment of alkyl cyanides ($R-CN$) is indeed greater than alkyl isocyanides ($R-NC$) because the $C \equiv N$ bond is more polar than the $N^+ \equiv C^-$ bond where formal charges oppose the electronegativity difference. However, the acidic hydrolysis of $R-NC$ produces a primary amine ($R-NH_2$) and formic acid ($HCOOH$), not a carboxylic acid ($R-COOH$). Thus, Statement I is false.
Statement II: $R-CN$ on acidic hydrolysis gives $R-COOH$. Treatment with $SOCl_2$ gives $R-COCl$. Addition of $NH_3$ gives $R-CONH_2$ (compound x). Treatment of $R-CONH_2$ with $NaOCl/NaOH$ (Hofmann Bromamide Degradation equivalent) gives $R-NH_2$. $R-NH_2$ on treatment with $CHCl_3/KOH/\Delta$ (Carbylamine reaction) produces $R-NC$. Thus, Statement II is true.
Therefore, Statement I is false but Statement II is true.

</details>

---

### Q16. JEE Main 2026 (24 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The correct stability order of the following diazonium salts is 
 <img style="width:400px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/24S1_q_63_1_v2.png">

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{C}>\mathrm{A}>\mathrm{D}>\mathrm{B}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{A}>\mathrm{C}>\mathrm{D}>\mathrm{B}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{A}>\mathrm{B}>\mathrm{C}>\mathrm{D}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{C}>\mathrm{D}>\mathrm{B}>\mathrm{A}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\mathrm{A}>\mathrm{C}>\mathrm{D}>\mathrm{B}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The stability of benzenediazonium salts is governed by the electronic effects of the substituents present on the benzene ring.
The diazonium group $-N_2^+$ is strongly electron-withdrawing. Electron-donating groups (EDG) stabilize the diazonium cation by dispersing the positive charge through resonance or inductive effects, whereas electron-withdrawing groups (EWG) destabilize it by increasing the positive charge density.
In (A), the $-OCH_3$ group at the para position is a strong electron-donating group via the $+M$ effect, which significantly stabilizes the diazonium ion.
In (C), there is no substituent (hydrogen), providing a baseline stability.
In (D), the $-CN$ group is an electron-withdrawing group via $-M$ and $-I$ effects, which destabilizes the cation.
In (B), the $-NO_2$ group is a very strong electron-withdrawing group via $-M$ and $-I$ effects, even stronger than $-CN$, leading to the least stability.
Comparing the effects: $+M$ of $-OCH_3$ (A) > $H$ (C) > $-M$ of $-CN$ (D) > $-M$ of $-NO_2$ (B).
Therefore, the correct stability order is $A > C > D > B$.

</details>

### Q18. JEE Main 2026 (23 January Shift 2)

A student has been given a compound " $x$ " of molecular formula- $\mathrm{C}_{6} \mathrm{H}_{7} \mathrm{~N}$. ' $x$ ' is sparingly soluble in water. However, on addition of dilute mineral acid, ' $x$ ' becomes soluble in water. ' $x$ ' when treated with $\mathrm{CHCl}_{3}$ and $\mathrm{KOH}($ alc $)$, ' $y$ ' is produced. ' y ' has a specific unpleasant smell. On treatment with benzenesulphonyl chloride, ' $x$ ' gives a compound ' $z$ ' which is soluble in alkali. The number of different " $H$ " atoms present in ' $z$ ' is:-

X with $\text{CHCl}_3 + \text{KOH (alc.)}$ gives Y with unpleasant smell → carbylamine reaction, confirms primary amine.
X with benzenesulphonyl chloride gives Z which is soluble in alkali → Hinsberg test confirms primary amine. Z is N-phenylbenzenesulfonamide.

### Q19. JEE Main 2026 (23 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Consider the following sequence of reactions. 
 <img style="width:500px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/23S1_q_61_1.png"> 
 Assuming that the reaction proceeds to completion, then 137 mg of 4 -nitrotoluene will produce $\_\_\_\_$ mg of $B$. (Given molar mass in $\mathrm{g} \mathrm{mol}^{-1} \mathrm{H}: 1, \mathrm{C}: 12, \mathrm{~N}: 14, \mathrm{O}: 16, \mathrm{Br}: 80$)

**Options:**

- **(A)** 208
- **(B)** 301
- **(C)** 228
- **(D)** 146

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 228**

**Detailed Solution:**

Starting with 137 mg of 4-nitrotoluene (C₇H₉NO₂, MW = 137).
Step 1: Reduction with Sn/HCl converts NO₂ to NH₂, producing 4-aminotoluene (A).
Step 2: Acetylation with (CH₃CO)₂O produces the acetamide intermediate, then bromination with Br₂/AcOH adds bromine to the para position (ortho-para directing due to acetamide group), yielding 4-bromo-N-(4-methylphenyl)acetamide (B) with molecular formula C₉H₁₀BrNO and MW = 228 g/mol.
Moles of starting material = 137 mg ÷ 137 g/mol = 1 mmol.
Assuming complete reaction, moles of B produced = 1 mmol.
Mass of B = 1 mmol × 228 mg/mmol = 228 mg.

</details>

### Q20. JEE Main 2026 (22 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The mass of benzanilide obtained from the benzoylation reaction of 5.8 g of aniline, if yield of product is $82 \%$, is $\_\_\_\_$ g (nearest integer). (Given molar mass in $\mathrm{g} \mathrm{mol}^{-1} \mathrm{H}: 1, \mathrm{C}: 12, \mathrm{~N}: 14, \mathrm{O}: 16$)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **10**

**Detailed Solution:**

Benzoylation of aniline:
$C_6H_5NH_2 + C_6H_5COCl \rightarrow C_6H_5CONHC_6H_5 + HCl$

Molar mass of aniline:
$M = 6(12) + 7(1) + 14 = 93$ g/mol

Moles of aniline:
$n = \frac{5.8}{93} = 0.0624$ mol

Molar mass of benzanilide ($C_{13}H_{11}NO$):
$M = 13(12) + 11(1) + 14 + 16 = 197$ g/mol

Theoretical mass of benzanilide:
$m_{theoretical} = 0.0624 \times 197 = 12.29$ g

Actual mass with 82% yield:
$m_{actual} = 12.29 \times 0.82 = 10.08 \approx 10$ g

</details>

### Q21. JEE Main 2026 (22 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

' A ' is a neutral organic compound ($\mathrm{M}. \mathrm{F}: \mathrm{C}_{8} \mathrm{H}_{9} \mathrm{ON}$). On treatment with aqueous $\mathrm{Br}_{2} / \mathrm{HO}^{(-)}$, ' A ' forms a compound ' B ' which is soluble in dilute acid. ' B ' on treatment with aqueous $\mathrm{NaNO}_{2} / \mathrm{HCl}\left(0-5^{\circ} \mathrm{C}\right)$ produces a compound ' C ' which on treatment with $\mathrm{CuCN} / \mathrm{NaCN}$ produces ' D '. Hydrolysis of ' D ' produces ' E ' which is also obtainable from the hydrolysis of ' A '. ' E ' on treatment with acidified $\mathrm{KMnO}_{4}$ produces ' $F$ '. ' $F$ ' contains two different types of hydrogen atoms. The structure of ' A ' is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img style="width:150px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/22S1_o_68_1_1.png">
- **(B)** <img style="width:150px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/22S1_o_68_2_1.png">
- **(C)** <img style="width:180px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/22S1_o_68_3_1.png">
- **(D)** <img style="width:200px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/22S1_o_68_4_1.png">

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <img style="width:180px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/22S1_o_68_3_1.png">**

**Detailed Solution:**

Compound A undergoes bromination with aqueous Br₂/HO⁻ to form B, which is soluble in dilute acid, indicating an amino group is present.
B undergoes diazotization with NaNO₂/HCl at 0-5°C to form diazonium compound C.
The diazonium compound is then treated with CaCN/NaOH to form nitrile compound D.
Hydrolysis of D produces carboxylic acid E.
When A is treated with acidified KMnO₄, it produces F containing two different types of hydrogen atoms.

This reaction sequence is characteristic of anthranilic acid (2-aminobenzoic acid), where the amino group directs bromination to the para position, and subsequent reactions proceed through the diazonium intermediate.

The structure shown in option (3), which is 2-aminobenzoic acid, fits all these transformations.

</details>

### Q22. JEE Main 2026 (21 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<img style="width:400px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/21S2_q_62_1.png"> 
 Consider the above sequence of reactions. The number of bromine atom(s) in the final product (P) will be :

**Options:**

- **(A)** 3
- **(B)** 5
- **(C)** 6
- **(D)** 1

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 5**

**Detailed Solution:**

The reaction sequence is as follows:
1. Bromination of nitrobenzene with $Br_2/FeBr_3$ results in meta-bromonitrobenzene because the $-NO_2$ group is meta-directing. (1 Br atom)
2. Reduction of the nitro group using $Sn/HCl$ followed by pH neutralization converts $-NO_2$ to $-NH_2$. The product is m-bromoaniline.
3. Reaction with $Br_2/H_2O$ (bromine water) on m-bromoaniline: The $-NH_2$ group is highly activating and ortho/para directing. In m-bromoaniline, the positions ortho and para to $-NH_2$ are vacant (positions 2, 4, and 6 relative to $-NH_2$). Bromine water causes polybromination at all available ortho and para positions. This adds 3 more bromine atoms. (Total 1 + 3 = 4 Br atoms)
4. Diazotization with $NaNO_2/HBr$ at $0-5^{\circ}C$ converts the $-NH_2$ group into a diazonium salt $-N_2^+Br^-$.
5. Sandmeyer reaction with $CuBr/NaBr$ replaces the diazonium group with a bromine atom. (Total 4 + 1 = 5 Br atoms)
The final product (P) is 1,2,3,4,5-pentabromobenzene. Therefore, the number of bromine atoms in the final product is 5.

</details>

### Q23. JEE Main 2026 (21 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Consider the following reaction sequence 
 <img style="width:600px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/21S1_q_71_1.png"> 
 The percentage of nitrogen in product ' T ' formed is $\_\_\_\_$%. (Nearest integer)
(Given molar mass in $\mathrm{g} \mathrm{mol}^{-1} \mathrm{H}: 1, \mathrm{C}: 12, \mathrm{~N}: 14, \mathrm{O}: 16$)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **20**

**Detailed Solution:**

Benzene $\xrightarrow{HNO_3/H_2SO_4}$ Nitrobenzene (P) $\xrightarrow{Sn/HCl}$ Aniline (Q) $\xrightarrow{(CH_3CO)_2O}$ Acetanilide (R)
R $\xrightarrow{HNO_3/H_2SO_4}$ p-Nitroacetanilide (S) $\xrightarrow{HCl/EtOH, \Delta}$ p-Nitroaniline (T)
T = p-Nitroaniline ($O_2N-C_6H_4-NH_2$), molecular formula $C_6H_6N_2O_2$
Molar mass = $6(12) + 6(1) + 2(14) + 2(16) = 72 + 6 + 28 + 32 = 138$ g/mol
% N = $\frac{28}{138} \times 100 = 20.29\%$
Nearest integer = 20

</details>

### Q24. JEE Main 2026 (21 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

An organic compound $(\mathrm{P})$ on treatment with aqueous ammonia under hot condition forms compound (Q) which on heating with $\mathrm{Br}_{2}$ and KOH forms compound (R) having molecular formula $\mathrm{C}_{6} \mathrm{H}_{7} \mathrm{~N}$. Names of P, Q and R respectively are.

**Options:**

- **(A)** Phenylethanoic acid, phenylethanamide, benzamine
- **(B)** Benzoic acid, 4-methylbenzamide, 4-methylaniline
- **(C)** Toluic acid, methylbenzamide, 2-methylaniline
- **(D)** Benzoic acid, benzamide, aniline

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Benzoic acid, benzamide, aniline**

**Detailed Solution:**

Q + $Br_2$/KOH → R is Hofmann bromamide degradation: Amide → Amine (with one less carbon).
R = $C_6H_7N$ = $C_6H_5NH_2$ = Aniline
For Q to give aniline, Q = Benzamide ($C_6H_5CONH_2$)
$C_6H_5CONH_2 \xrightarrow{Br_2/KOH} C_6H_5NH_2$
P + hot $NH_3$ → Q: Carboxylic acid + $NH_3$ → Amide
P = Benzoic acid ($C_6H_5COOH$)
$C_6H_5COOH \xrightarrow{NH_3, \Delta} C_6H_5CONH_2$
P = Benzoic acid, Q = Benzamide, R = Aniline

</details>

### Q25. JEE Main 2026 (21 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

A hydrocarbon ' $\mathrm{P}^{\prime}\left(\mathrm{C}_{4} \mathrm{H}_{8}\right)$ on reaction with HCl gives an optically active compound ' Q ' $\left(\mathrm{C}_{4} \mathrm{H}_{9} \mathrm{Cl}\right)$ which on reaction with one mole of ammonia gives compound ' $\mathrm{R}^{\prime}\left(\mathrm{C}_{4} \mathrm{H}_{11} \mathrm{~N}\right)$. ' $\mathrm{R}^{\prime}$ on diazotization followed by hydrolysis gives ' S '.
Identify $\mathrm{P}, \mathrm{Q}, \mathrm{R}$ and S.

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img style="width:400px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/21S1_o_59_1_1.png">
- **(B)** <img style="width:600px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/21S1_o_59_2_1.png">
- **(C)** <img style="width:600px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/21S1_o_59_3_1.png">
- **(D)** <img style="width:600px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/21S1_o_59_4_1.png">

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <img style="width:600px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/21S1_o_59_4_1.png">**

**Detailed Solution:**

For Q ($C_4H_9Cl$) to be optically active, it must have a chiral center.
P ($C_4H_8$) = but-2-ene ($CH_3-CH=CH-CH_3$)
P + HCl → Q: $CH_3-CH=CH-CH_3 + HCl \rightarrow CH_3-CH(Cl)-CH_2-CH_3$ (2-chlorobutane, chiral at C-2)
Q + $NH_3$ → R: 2-chlorobutane + $NH_3 \rightarrow CH_3-CH(NH_2)-CH_2-CH_3$ (2-aminobutane)
R → diazotization → hydrolysis → S: 2-aminobutane $\xrightarrow{NaNO_2/HCl}$ diazonium salt $\xrightarrow{H_2O}$ $CH_3-CH(OH)-CH_2-CH_3$ (butan-2-ol)

</details>

### Q34. JEE Main 2025 (2 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The correct order of basic nature on aqueous solution for the bases \(\mathrm{NH}_3, \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{~N}-\mathrm{NH}_2, \mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{CH}_2 \mathrm{NH}_2\), \(\left(\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{CH}_2\right)_2 \mathrm{NH}\) and \(\left(\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{CH}_2\right)_3 \mathrm{~N}\) is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** \(\mathrm{NH}_3  \lt \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{~N}-\mathrm{NH}_2 \lt \left(\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{CH}_2\right)_3 \mathrm{~N} \lt \) \(\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{CH}_2 \mathrm{NH}_2 \lt \left(\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{CH}_2\right)_2 \mathrm{NH}\)
- **(B)** \(\mathrm{NH}_3  \lt \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{~N}-\mathrm{NH}_2 \lt \mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{CH}_2 \mathrm{NH}_2 \lt \) \(\left(\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{CH}_2\right)_2 \mathrm{NH} \lt \left(\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{CH}_2\right)_3 \mathrm{~N}\)
- **(C)** \(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{~N}-\mathrm{NH}_2  \lt \mathrm{NH}_3 \lt \left(\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{CH}_2\right)_3 \mathrm{~N} \lt \) \(\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{CH}_2 \mathrm{NH}_2 \lt \left(\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{CH}_2\right)_2 \mathrm{NH}\)
- **(D)** \(\mathrm{NH}_2-\mathrm{NH}_2  \lt \mathrm{NH}_3 \lt \mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{CH}_2 \mathrm{NH}_2 \lt \) \(\left(\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{CH}_2\right)_3 \mathrm{~N} \lt \left(\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{CH}_2\right)_2 \mathrm{NH}\)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) \(\mathrm{NH}_2-\mathrm{NH}_2  \lt \mathrm{NH}_3 \lt \mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{CH}_2 \mathrm{NH}_2 \lt \) \(\left(\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{CH}_2\right)_3 \mathrm{~N} \lt \left(\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{CH}_2\right)_2 \mathrm{NH}\)**

**Detailed Solution:**

Basic strength of amine depends on hydrogen bonding and electronic inductive effect.
\(\mathrm{NH}(\mathrm{Et})_2  \gt  \mathrm{N}(\mathrm{Et})_3  \gt  \mathrm{NH}_2 \mathrm{Et}  \gt  \mathrm{\stackrel{\cdot \cdot}{N}H}_3  \gt  \mathrm{NH}_2-\mathrm{NH}_2\)

</details>

### Q35. JEE Main 2025 (29 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given below are some nitrogen containing compounds
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/JCVcFj4GZqdYY5WkIyAtFqmO6r9fWp-MBJHX6yJ6uLg.original.fullsize.png"/>

Each of them is treated with HCl separately. 1.0 g of the most basic compound will consume _______ mg of HCl.
(Given molar mass in \(\mathrm{g} \mathrm{mol}^{-1} \mathrm{C}: 12, \mathrm{H}: 1, \mathrm{O}: 16, \mathrm{Cl}: 35.5\))

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **341**

**Detailed Solution:**

Benzyl Amine is most basic due to localised lone pair.


<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/Z2w2wnuK0W8o84ZQifPeVCcfE8IjkFjsTQa7i1Yu0Q8.original.fullsize.png">




Mole of benzyl Amine $\Rightarrow \frac{1}{107}=0.00934$ mole
1 Mole of Benzyl amine consumed 1 mole of HCl
So, Mole of HCl consumed $\rightarrow 0.00934$ mole
Mass of HCl consumed $\rightarrow 0.00934 \times$ molar mass
of HCl
$\begin{aligned}
& =0.00934 \times 36.5 \\
& =0.341 \mathrm{gm} \\
& =341 \mathrm{mg}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q48. JEE Main 2024 (06 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

\(9.3 \mathrm{~g}\) of pure aniline upon diazotisation followed by coupling with phenol gives an orange dye. The mass of orange dye produced (assume 100\% yield/conversion) is _______ g. (nearest integer)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **20**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/fNGE9InE88mZQTCGES3BJXGjc63r84cr-r6gQ_MhcGw.original.fullsize.png">
Reaction suggests that 1 mole of aniline give 1 mole of orange dye.
$\begin{aligned}
& \text { so }(\mathrm{mol})_{\text {aniline }}=(\mathrm{mole})_{\text {orange dye }} \\
& \frac{9.3 \mathrm{~g}}{93 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}}=\frac{\text { mass of orange dye }}{199 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}}
\end{aligned}$
mass of orange dye $=19.9 \mathrm{~g} \simeq 20 \mathrm{~g}$

</details>

### Q50. JEE Main 2024 (04 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

From \(6.55 \mathrm{~g}\) of aniline, the maximum amount of acetanilide that can be prepared will be ______ \(\times 10^{-1} \mathrm{~g}\).

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

$95 \times 10^{-1}$

### Q53. JEE Main 2024 (04 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

\(\mathrm{X} \mathrm{g}\) of ethylamine is subjected to reaction with \(\mathrm{NaNO}_2 / \mathrm{HCl}\) followed by water; evolved dinitrogen gas which occupied \(2.24 \mathrm{~L}\) volume at STP. \(\mathrm{X}\) is \(\qquad\) \(\times 10^{-1} \mathrm{~g}\).

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **45**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\underset{\substack{\text { Mol.wt.45g }}}{\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{CH}_2 \mathrm{NH}_2} \xrightarrow{\mathrm{NaNO}_2+\mathrm{HCl}} \xrightarrow{\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}} \mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{CH}_2-\mathrm{OH}+\underset{14 \mathrm{~g}}{\mathrm{~N}_2}$
given : $\mathrm{N}_2$ evolved is 2.24 L i.e. 0.1 mole.
i.e. $\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{CH}_2 \mathrm{NH}_2$ (ethyl amine) will be $4.5 \mathrm{~g}$
$(=0.1 \mathrm{~mole})$
Hence the answer $=45 \times 10^{-1} \mathrm{~g}$

</details>

### Q54. JEE Main 2024 (01 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p>Given below are two statements:
Statement (I) : The <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> group in Aniline is ortho and para directing and a powerful activating group.
Statement (II) : Aniline does not undergo FriedelCraft's reaction (alkylation and acylation).</p><p>In the light of the above statements, choose the most appropriate answer from the options given below:</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are correct
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are incorrect
- **(C)** Statement I is incorrect but Statement II is correct
- **(D)** Statement I is correct but Statement II is incorrect

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Both Statement I and Statement II are correct**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is a highly activating group in organic chemistry due to its electron-donating nature. It is often used in synthetic chemistry to increase the reactivity of aromatic compounds towards electrophilic substitution reactions.</p><p>Aniline forms salt with the Lewis acid catalyst i.e., <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>AlCl</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>, which is used in Friedel-Crafts reaction.
Nitrogen of aniline acquires a positive charge and hence acts as a strong deactivating group for further reaction.</p><p>The <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> group in Aniline is ortho and para directing and a powerful activating group as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> has strong <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math> effect.</p><p>Hence, both statements are correct.</p>

</details>

---

### Q55. JEE Main 2024 (31 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

A compound <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>x</mi></mfenced></math> with molar mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>108</mn><mi>g</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> undergoes acetylation to give product with molar mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>192</mn><mi>g</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. The number of amino groups in the compound <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>x</mi></mfenced></math> is ________.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The compounds like amines and alcohols undergo acetylation with acetyl chloride. During this reaction, one proton of amine or alcohol is replaced with one acetyl group(<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mi>CO</mi><mo>-</mo></math>).</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/1aa3087f-9e8f-41ac-a5e3-23b74a289492.png" style="width: 600px; height: 107px;"/></p><p>The molar mass of the acetyl group is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>43</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi></math>. The increase in molar mass due to one acetyl group is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>43</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>42</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi></math>.</p><p>The molar mass of reactant is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>108</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi></math>. The molar mass of product is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>192</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi></math>.  The change in mass is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>84</mn><mo>.</mo></math></p><p>Hence, the number of acetyl groups <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>84</mn><mn>42</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q56. JEE Main 2024 (31 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Given below are two statements :</p><p>Statement I: Aniline reacts with con. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> followed by heating at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>453</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>473</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math> gives p-aminobenzene sulphonic acid, which gives blood red colour in the 'Lassaigne's test'.</p><p>Statement II: In Friedel - Craft's alkylation and acylation reactions, aniline forms salt with the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>AlCl</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> catalyst. Due to this, nitrogen of aniline acquires a positive charge and acts as deactivating group.</p><p>In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below :</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** Statement I is false but statement II is true
- **(B)** Both statement I and statement II are false
- **(C)** Statement I is true but statement II is false
- **(D)** Both statement I and statement II are true

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Both statement I and statement II are true**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Aniline reacts with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>conc</mi><mo>.</mo><mo> </mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math>followed by heating at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>453</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>473</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>gives p-aminobenzene sulphonic acid, which gives blood red colour in the 'Lassaigne's test'Nitrogen, sulphur, and halogens present in organic compounds are detected by Lassaigne's test. Here, a small piece of Na metal is heated in a fusion tube with the organic compound. The principle is that, in doing so, Na converts all the elements present into ionic form.</p><p>Aniline does not undergo Friedel-Crafts reaction (alkylation and acetylation) due to salt formation with aluminium chloride, the Lewis acid, which is used as a catalyst. Due to this, nitrogen of aniline acquires positive charge and hence acts as a strong deactivating group for further reaction.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/bd43543a-f730-410a-bc33-f10b5ce6c646.png" style="width: 1000px; height: 203px;"/></p>

</details>

### Q57. JEE Main 2024 (30 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p>The products A and B formed in the following reaction scheme are respectively</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/1a74ed18-37cf-43c1-871f-1f15c5686fd9.png" style="width: 650px; height: 146px;"/></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/dc6d5a03-fd02-4c9e-9c59-2e62d0f45aba.png" style="width: 500px; height: 142px;"/>
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/2fbc8730-46a4-4c23-9133-88adb39e6ada.png" style="width: 480px; height: 193px;"/>
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/88ac820e-2b20-4714-899e-33dfe83365fe.png" style="width: 599px; height: 120px;"/>
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f82024d1-0c81-47aa-8b50-e57a3cd158bb.png" style="width: 502px; height: 158px;"/>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/88ac820e-2b20-4714-899e-33dfe83365fe.png" style="width: 599px; height: 120px;"/>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Benzene reacts with concentrated nitric acid at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>323</mn><mo>-333 </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math> in the presence of concentrated sulphuric acid to form nitrobenzene. This converts to Aniline on reaction with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Sn</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>HCl</mi></math>.</p><p>The chemical process used in converting a primary aromatic amine into the corresponding diazonium salt of the amine is commonly referred to as diazotization by using sodium nitrite and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>HCl</mi></math>. This on reaction with phenol leads to diazotisation coupling.</p><p> </p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/a2870f47-88a7-4bac-b6a8-c9452676f954.png" style="width: 700px; height: 349px;"/></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q58. JEE Main 2024 (30 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p>The final product <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>, formed in the following multistep reaction sequence is:</p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/bc6d09d5-66b1-41ba-9223-1b93fa9e93d2.png" style="width: 400px; height: 163px;"/>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/7b5a4b6e-79d8-44b8-9069-3e9462557990.png" style="width: 220px; height: 130px;"/>
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/b06c67f8-bcaf-4bdd-aa5b-8e6d300c5321.png" style="width: 220px; height: 156px;"/>
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/106db6e6-aa09-4e50-911f-ba55e5eb568e.png" style="width: 200px; height: 131px;"/>
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/3e0b1f13-466d-433c-b721-c73a6c2d75bb.png" style="width: 200px; height: 137px;"/>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/b06c67f8-bcaf-4bdd-aa5b-8e6d300c5321.png" style="width: 220px; height: 156px;"/>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Bromobenzene reacts with magnesium in dry ether (diethyl ether) to form phenylmagnesium bromide, often abbreviated as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>PhMgBr</mi></math>.</p><p>The resulting phenylmagnesium bromide reacts with carbon dioxide in the presence of an acidic medium to form benzoic acid.</p><p>Heating benzoic acid with ammonia can lead to a reaction known as the Schotten-Baumann type reaction, which is a method for the amidation of carboxylic acids. In this reaction, the carboxylic acid group of benzoic acid reacts with ammonia  to form an amide, benzamide, under heating conditions.</p><p>When an amide is treated with bromine in an aqueous or ethanolic solution of sodium hydroxide, degradation of amide takes place leading to the formation of primary amine.</p><p>In this case, it is an hoffmann bromamide reaction, aniline is formed.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/5f5099a4-2f1a-4739-add8-a4a293f7c4da.png" style="width: 668px; height: 300px;"/></p>

</details>

---

### Q59. JEE Main 2024 (29 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>The product <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> formed in the following reaction is:</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/b81f5dc5-309c-4f75-956f-ff4b7ee8266c.png" style="width: 500px; height: 145px;"/></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f947d925-f9c6-4b09-85ea-fff86a45ebd6.png" style="width: 200px; height: 145px;"/>
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/6ad8047c-dc47-4598-b11d-171d1933e321.png" style="width: 200px; height: 140px;"/>
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/27640098-3dda-4f95-b025-5974806736fa.png" style="width: 208px; height: 163px;"/>
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d48e9a3f-21df-4d78-8de3-833733994358.png" style="width: 200px; height: 151px;"/>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/27640098-3dda-4f95-b025-5974806736fa.png" style="width: 208px; height: 163px;"/>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Diazotization: The process of conversion of a primary aromatic amino compound into a diazonium salt is known as diazotization. This process is carried out by adding an aqueous solution of sodium nitrite to a solution of primary aromatic amine (e.g., aniline) in excess of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>HCl</mi></math> at a temperature below <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mn>5</mn><mo>°</mo></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>.</p><p>Decomposition of benzene diazonium chloride by using <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Cu</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>/</mo><mi>HCl</mi></math> to form chlorobenzene is known as sandmeyer's reaction. <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/5910a9a3-7216-4023-b111-2bc664e9450c.png" style="width: 600px; height: 187px;"/></p>

</details>

### Q60. JEE Main 2024 (29 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p>Which of the following reaction is correct?</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/bc6db938-26be-45a3-9823-2ee48c951f7f.png" style="width: 504px; height: 44px;"/>
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/448cde91-e1d6-4657-a751-54838a61e471.png" style="width: 447px; height: 127px;"/>
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/07c64d4f-4141-4977-8c55-be7b237be1e0.png" style="width: 433px; height: 136px;"/>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><msub><mi>CONH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>Br</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>NaOH</mi><mo>→</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>Na</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>CO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>NaBr</mi><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/448cde91-e1d6-4657-a751-54838a61e471.png" style="width: 447px; height: 127px;"/>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The Markovnikov Rule predicts the regiochemistry of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>HX</mi></math> addition to unsymmetrically substituted alkenes. The halide component of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>HX</mi></math> bonds preferentially at the more highly substituted carbon, whereas hydrogen prefers the carbon which already contains more hydrogen.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/72560526-edb1-4f0c-972a-749c7f33fea3.png" style="width: 427px; height: 119px;"/></p><p>By the reaction with<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>HNO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> amine doesnot lose any carbon while producing alcohol.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mover><mo>→</mo><mrow><msub><mi>HNO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>/</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>-</mo><msup><mn>5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mrow></mover><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>OH</mi><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>HCl</mi></math></p><p>Cyclohexene undergo allylic substitution reaction with bromine in the presence of sunlight.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/7e454d13-fa21-4b3e-a29d-b2b6e98b14a4.png" style="width: 295px; height: 166px;"/></p><p>In Hoffmann bromamide degradation amides give amines by loss of one carbon.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><msub><mi>CONH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>Br</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>NaOH</mi><mover><mo>→</mo><mrow></mrow></mover><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>Na</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>CO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>NaBr</mi><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q61. JEE Main 2023 (15 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/7de3b2b0-487f-45d0-b739-7231f186d89b.png" style="width: 381px; height: 169px;" /></p><p>In the above conversion the correct sequence of reagents to be added is</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>KMnO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi>ii</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>Br</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>/</mo><mi>Fe</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi>iii</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Fe</mi><mo>/</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi>iv</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>Br</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>/</mo><mi>Fe</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi>ii</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Fe</mi><mo>/</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi>iii</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>KMnO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi>iv</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Fe</mi><mo>/</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi>ii</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>HONO</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi>iii</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>CuCl</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi>iv</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>KMnO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>Br</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>Br</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>/</mo><mi>Fe</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi>ii</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Fe</mi><mo>/</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi>iii</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>HONO</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi>iv</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>CuCl</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>KMnO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>Br</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>/</mo><mi>Fe</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi>ii</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Fe</mi><mo>/</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi>iii</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>HONO</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi>iv</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>CuCl</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>KMnO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The required product can be obtained by the following method. In this method nitro toluene is subjected to bromination, bromo group is substituted at ortho position to the methyl as it is more activating group. Then nitro group is reduced to amine group with iron metal in acid. The amine group undergoes diazotisation with nitrous acid. The diazonium group is replaced with chloro group in the presence of cuprous chloride. Finally, methyl group is oxidised in the presence of potassium permanganate.</p><p> </p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/1d976a4f-ce13-4918-bcc8-8f105cfe3527.png" style="width: 597px; height: 331px;" /></p>

</details>

### Q62. JEE Main 2023 (15 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Consider the following sequence of reactions</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/9af31638-2bfd-4638-a85f-20a42c3c47f0.png" style="width: 553px; height: 95px;" /></p><p>The product &lsquo;<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math>&rsquo; is</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/580b9c54-0c04-43e8-a367-d8ebb460a38c.png" style="width: 421px; height: 129px;" /></p>
- **(B)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/3576975d-61d5-44e0-96a8-96266ff3f443.png" style="width: 429px; height: 123px;" /></p>
- **(C)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/0d0346ca-5c02-4784-86de-3bd4ea7739b2.png" style="width: 453px; height: 108px;" /></p>
- **(D)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/2b033885-4516-4f55-bef7-5619bef110c6.png" style="width: 421px; height: 112px;" /></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/0d0346ca-5c02-4784-86de-3bd4ea7739b2.png" style="width: 453px; height: 108px;" /></p>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The reaction of aniline (aromatic amine) with nitrous acid results in the diazonium salt formation which is benzene diazonium chloride.When benzene diazonium chloride reacts with N, N - dimethyl aniline at ice cold condition gives p - dimethyl aminoazobenzene. It is an example of coupling reaction.</p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/59b0b118-3c19-4176-9fa4-be97b865961f.png" style="width: 758px; height: 206px;" /></p>

</details>

### Q63. JEE Main 2023 (13 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Compound A from the following reaction sequence is :</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d4154d09-cc6a-4bf4-b43b-49cef98f9350.png" style="width: 450px; height: 109px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** Benzoic Acid
- **(B)** Aniline
- **(C)** Salicylic Acid
- **(D)** Phenol

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Phenol**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>When aniline treated with bromine, the reaction results in the formation of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>6</mn></math>-tribromo phenylamine. This reaction is an example of bromination. The process of conversion of a primary aromatic amino compound into a diazonium salt is known as diazotization. This process is carried out by adding an aqueous solution of sodium nitrite to a solution of primary aromatic amine (e.g., aniline) in excess of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>HCl</mi></math> at a temperature below <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>. Benzene diazonium chloride reacts with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> in aqueous medium to form benzene.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/a2e395e0-1c37-4a81-bf2f-61db28911458.png" style="width: 500px; height: 291px;" /></p>

</details>

---

### Q64. JEE Main 2023 (11 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p>Compoun <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#39;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#39;</mo></math> is
<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d2ac9179-fa34-41c9-a663-af7de92b6c7f.png" style="width: 434px; height: 115px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/9d59557d-4fa2-4244-b96c-f3abea910163.png" style="width: 180px; height: 133px;" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/7fb5778f-c636-48a4-bff0-eea05c9232d4.png" style="width: 180px; height: 170px;" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/c45bdbb0-80f2-4081-8308-32687d5dcc27.png" style="width: 130px; height: 164px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/c23f46ef-0e0d-4cd4-ba72-f496e25282fe.png" style="width: 180px; height: 142px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/9d59557d-4fa2-4244-b96c-f3abea910163.png" style="width: 180px; height: 133px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Nitrous acid gives nitrosonium ion as electrophile (<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>NO</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math>) which attacks phenol at less hindered p-position of form p-nitrosophenol.  The nitroso group undergo reduction and forms amine in the presence of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mi>SH</mi></math>.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/bec81fab-2ffa-4d87-9b53-ba3853458d09.png" style="width: 613px; height: 112px;" /></p>

</details>

---

### Q65. JEE Main 2023 (11 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/453baaa5-ec9e-42b6-9bdb-40d31dbb5c1b-image.png" style="width: 399px; height: 74px;" /></p><p>&#39;X&#39;</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/e02130cd-9b72-4a27-95c2-eb447069abea-image.png" style="width: 150px; height: 149px;" /></p>
- **(B)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/1187732b-79be-45b4-9285-6640eecd1518-image.png" style="width: 150px; height: 119px;" /></p>
- **(C)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/9557e4cb-5a43-4ad4-b55e-78802882f360-image.png" style="width: 150px; height: 126px;" /></p>
- **(D)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/105d16e3-1faa-41db-a568-966a2a7ac207-image.png" style="width: 200px; height: 107px;" /></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/e02130cd-9b72-4a27-95c2-eb447069abea-image.png" style="width: 150px; height: 149px;" /></p>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Benzotriazole can be prepared by treating o-phenylenediamine with nitrous acid (liberated during the reaction between sodium nitrite and acetic acid) to form mono-diazonium salt that follows spontaneous intramolecular cyclization reaction to produce benzotriazole.<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/19b16302-9081-433c-9f48-8e303f4cc769-image.png" style="width: 400px; height: 278px;" /></p>

</details>

### Q66. JEE Main 2023 (08 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p>The product <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mo>)</mo></math> formed from the following multistep reaction is:</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ec747d72-8e43-444f-bb58-43d72b39df44.png" style="width: 540px; height: 125px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/deaa8bbc-462b-4d2d-a60b-798729d38635-image.png" style="width: 200px; height: 116px;" /></p>
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/1650029c-58f1-4b90-b544-cc47c231b7cc-image.png" style="width: 200px; height: 123px;" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f4dcb173-0be1-434e-97bf-b8f1ca321b2d-image.png" style="width: 179px; height: 137px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/7442b26d-e131-4d38-93e3-7f9aacf13a4f-image.png" style="width: 200px; height: 134px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f4dcb173-0be1-434e-97bf-b8f1ca321b2d-image.png" style="width: 179px; height: 137px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The first step of the sequence is involved with electrophilic substitution reaction. Nitro group is deactivating group and methyl group is activating group, hence, the substitution reaction takes place with respect to methyl group. In the second step nitro group is reduced to amine group with hydrogen in the presence of palladium metal. The aniline undergo diazotisation in the third step.The diazonium salt loses nitrogen gas in the presence of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>.</p><p> </p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/6e1be6a5-bf4a-4b55-944a-1e9fcf35c6e1.png" style="width: 640px; height: 245px;" /></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q67. JEE Main 2023 (08 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p>Match List I with List II :</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/86f784f5-7087-4a18-8e47-45cb9f1d5a23.png" style="width: 150px; height: 109px;" /> s reacted with reagents in List I to form products in List II</p><p>products in List II.</p><table border="1" cellpadding="1" cellspacing="1" style="width: 500px;">	<tbody>		<tr>			<td colspan="4">List I (Reagent)                                                       List II (Product)</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>A.</td>			<td><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/e9b7ca1e-a08c-463c-befd-bad3cae01260.png" style="width: 150px; height: 108px;" /></td>			<td>I.</td>			<td><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ab91210b-b62c-4fa2-8d6c-44f929671b1d.png" style="width: 130px; height: 115px;" /></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>B.</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>HBF</mi><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#916;</mi></mrow></msub></math></td>			<td>II.</td>			<td><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/52eff05c-a27b-44e0-ac32-0c0af363c271.png" style="width: 140px; height: 102px;" /></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>C.</td>			<td>Cu, HCl</td>			<td>III.</td>			<td><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ebd6b0d7-3841-4ec9-a265-fb22ce59a5e8.png" style="width: 300px; height: 69px;" /></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>D.</td>			<td>CuCN/KCN</td>			<td>IV.</td>			<td><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ff994660-4747-4109-9052-2ad0fdc4d27c.png" style="width: 150px; height: 116px;" /></td>		</tr>	</tbody></table>Choose the correct answer from the options given below:<p> </p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** A-IV, B-III, C-II, D-I
- **(B)** A-III, B-I, C-II, D-IV
- **(C)** A-I, B-III, C-IV, D-II
- **(D)** A-III, B-I, C-IV, D-II

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) A-III, B-I, C-IV, D-II**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Coupling reaction between benzene diazonium chlorides with aniline in acidic medium takes place to give yellow colour. In this reaction the nitrogen <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> in the diazonium ion is lost and forms the bridge between two benzene rings forming an electrophilic substitution reaction.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/c3fa8f4d-49df-41ea-b1a1-4283c2e002c1.png" style="width: 508px; height: 114px;" /></p><p>Fluoro benzene is prepared by treating benzene diazonium chloride with fluoro boric acid. This reaction produces diazonium fluoroborate which on heating produces fluorobenzene. This reaction is called Balz-Schiemann reaction.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/1655d39b-ee53-4d26-87d9-b61330652897.png" style="width: 354px; height: 135px;" /></p><p>Gattermann reaction is used for obtaining chlorobenzene from benzenediazonium chloride by treating it with Cu/HCl  respectively.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/72526328-345d-4304-b672-f1ec5dc2b099.png" style="width: 359px; height: 124px;" /></p><p>Similarly, Benzene diazonium chloride solution on reaction with cuprous cyanide and potassium cyanide gives benzonitrile. This reaction is known as Sandmeyer&#39;s reaction.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/3d46c0d7-e4a8-4986-9ef9-f4f5e06c7349.png" style="width: 328px; height: 110px;" /></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q68. JEE Main 2023 (06 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Number of isomeric aromatic amines with molecular formula <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>8</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>11</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>, which can be synthesized by Gabriel Phthalimide synthesis is _____.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **5**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>8</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>11</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math></p><p>Degree of unsaturation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math>, The degree of unsaturation indicates the total number of pi bonds and rings within a molecule which makes it easier for one to figure out the molecular structure</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/dbc28ae3-01f6-4387-af5e-2f692ac9e6ed-image.png" style="width: 500px; height: 448px;" /></p><p>So, number of aromatic amines <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math></p></blockquote>

</details>
