### Q69. JEE Main 2023 (06 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/395312f8-a3e5-40f1-b1b0-3061f12a7007-image.png" style="width: 500px; height: 149px;" /></p><p>Compound <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math> is neutral, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi></math> gives effervescence with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NaHCO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> while <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math> reacts with Hinsberg&rsquo;s reagent to give solid soluble in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaOH</mi><mo>.</mo></math> Compound <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math> is</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/40268a74-c8cb-483e-a89d-c8e7a79b8d7f-image.png" style="width: 250px; height: 104px;" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/30b90ce4-b4ee-408b-8f60-2cdce48fc493-image.png" style="width: 250px; height: 121px;" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/de4343ca-cab9-422e-932a-e9d3cd38e2b8-image.png" style="width: 250px; height: 128px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/7d869001-cea6-4443-a1d3-a8cf858c543f-image.png" style="width: 250px; height: 109px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/7d869001-cea6-4443-a1d3-a8cf858c543f-image.png" style="width: 250px; height: 109px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi></math> is giving effervescence with sodium bicarbonate, it means <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi></math> contain carboxylic acid functional group. The compound <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math> is reacting with Hinsberg&#39;s reagent and giving solid which soluble in sodium hydroxide means <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math> contain primary amine functional group. Hence, The compound <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></math>can be a secondary amide. The overall reaction is shown below.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/4da61d8b-93ad-41a2-809d-f1e74ff24b95-image.png" style="width: 600px; height: 165px;" /></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q70. JEE Main 2023 (06 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>The major product formed in the following reaction is</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ea562a05-cd9a-497e-b8f5-36b1af9d9343-image.png" style="width: 300px; height: 88px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/3e4c30e9-ab19-4cf3-a9b0-2b9468f8fff2-image.png" style="width: 150px; height: 114px;" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/25ba9578-f9e6-45c2-bf16-435550e30a7e-image.png" style="width: 150px; height: 105px;" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/6bc0477e-03b3-4516-8830-d0bbf821205c-image.png" style="width: 150px; height: 115px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/2c3b7417-6664-4fe3-96dd-bb5767ed12c7-image.png" style="width: 150px; height: 115px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/25ba9578-f9e6-45c2-bf16-435550e30a7e-image.png" style="width: 150px; height: 105px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>When an amide is treated with bromine in an aqueous or ethanolic solution of sodium hydroxide, degradation of amide takes place leading to the formation of primary amine. This reaction involves the degradation of amide and is popularly known as Hoffmann bromamide degradation reaction. In the given molecule, amide part convert to amine with bromine in sodium hydroxide. Now, nucleophilic acyl substitution reaction takes place to get the final product as shown below.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/594a6a5e-d21b-4052-8447-b36df9047e2e-image.png" style="width: 500px; height: 331px;" /></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q71. JEE Main 2023 (01 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>In the following reaction, &lsquo;<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>&rsquo; is</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/bc1c3541-050b-4e5b-8c82-8c90ee56b857-9896779.png" style="width: 500px; height: 244px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/20e4e26a-9131-421c-a1e0-16da51779cd8-9896779_A.png" style="width: 300px; height: 235px;" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ef118372-9de9-4b77-a320-9776e779def0-9896779_B.png" style="width: 300px; height: 240px;" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/6f40a272-6222-43be-afd7-ef36ef155e64-9896779_C.png" style="width: 300px; height: 240px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/b2140965-e9cc-4fbe-819f-d137fa0ec047-9896779_D.png" style="width: 300px; height: 226px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ef118372-9de9-4b77-a320-9776e779def0-9896779_B.png" style="width: 300px; height: 240px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given reactant undergoes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>2</mn></math> reaction with diethyl carbonate.</p><p>Initially lone pair electron of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8211;</mo><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> attack on electrophilic carbon, after then lone pair electron of oxygen attacks leading to formation of cyclic compound.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ffb95a1d-c068-44ac-b1ad-cbefa7dc62fe-9896779_S.png" style="width: 550px; height: 520px;" /> </p>

</details>

### Q72. JEE Main 2023 (31 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

An organic compound <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>[</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>]</mo><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>11</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>, shows optical activity and gives <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> gas on treatment with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>HNO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>. The compound <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>[</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>]</mo></math> reacts with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>PhSO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>Cl</mi></math> producing a compound which is soluble in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KOH</mi></math>. The structure of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/1d740b47-401c-464f-96dd-4c1b1ddb1400-9896310.PNG" style="width: 202px; height: 50px;" /></p>
- **(B)** <p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/6f58e0da-5ec7-4fbc-944b-e0f8d623a95a-9896310_1.PNG" style="width: 122px; height: 160px;" /></p>
- **(C)** <p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/c006bef2-2514-409a-8cd1-e9357233cd31-9896310_2.PNG" style="width: 175px; height: 160px;" /></p>
- **(D)** <p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/bd5143be-9da6-4584-ac72-18dd7c505a91-9896310_3.PNG" style="width: 134px; height: 70px;" /></p><p> </p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/bd5143be-9da6-4584-ac72-18dd7c505a91-9896310_3.PNG" style="width: 134px; height: 70px;" /></p><p> </p>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given:
1. Compound is optically active <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo></math> contains chiral carbon/Nitrogen.
2. Compound liberates <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> gas with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>HNO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#8658;</mo></math> It is primary amine
3. Compound reacts with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>PhSO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>Cl</mi></math> and the product is soluble in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KOH</mi></math> - It is a primary amine.</p><p>Option (A) and (D) are primary amines. But only option (D) satisfy all the above conditions.</p>

</details>

### Q73. JEE Main 2023 (31 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p>How many of the transformation given below would result in aromatic amines?</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/0168daf9-5456-4cdd-bf14-1a947873a4da-9896183_3.png" style="width: 500px; height: 564px;" /></p><p> </p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **3**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Products in the given reactions are as follows:</p><p>1. Hoffmann bromamide degradation is useful in the production of aniline as it converts the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>CONH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> functional group into the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> group. So, we need to take a starting material that has the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>CONH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> group and after getting converted into <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> group, the molecule obtained is aniline. So, in order to obtain aniline as the final product, we require Benzamide as a starting material. So, in this way, we can easily convert Benzamide to Aniline.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/3b3bad20-6680-447a-97a4-3f7bd238d279-9896183.png" style="width: 500px; height: 105px;" /></p><p>2. Gabriel phthalimide synthesis cannot be used to prepare aniline because aryl halides do not give Gabriel phthalimide reaction.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/01dc03c0-9049-46a0-9d62-995e7b892caa-image.png" style="width: 321px; height: 130px;" /></p><p>3. When nitrobenzene is reacted with an active metal like Pd/C and H<sub>2</sub><sub>,</sub>  it produces aniline. This reaction is also called Nitrobenzene reduction.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/da2c02d8-7d00-40bb-97ff-e50f3bea7704-9896183_4.png" style="width: 500px; height: 114px;" /></p><p>4. When acetanilide is treated with dil. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math>, it produces aniline.</p><p> </p><p>Aromatic amines will be formed in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>4</mn></math>.</p><p>So, answer is 3.</p><p> </p>

</details>

### Q74. JEE Main 2023 (31 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/c66879a5-8742-4b8e-894b-c962737f75ba-9896191.PNG" style="width: 406px; height: 120px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/68b150f1-0aca-41d0-969b-1f1a09352507-9896191_1.PNG" style="width: 276px; height: 180px;" />
- **(B)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/7e660dad-ae2b-4edd-8434-2eeac95e2d81-9896191_3.PNG" style="width: 279px; height: 180px;" />
- **(C)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/7715cc96-6b90-4637-bbad-c7e6d84f0ef6-9896191_2.PNG" style="width: 319px; height: 180px;" />
- **(D)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/db2fd976-b8ae-4f15-a0fd-bbb308160a3e-9896191_4.PNG" style="width: 263px; height: 180px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/db2fd976-b8ae-4f15-a0fd-bbb308160a3e-9896191_4.PNG" style="width: 263px; height: 180px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Nitrobenzene is reduced to aniline by passing hydrogen gas in presence of finely divided palladium and also by reduction with metals in an acidic medium. When aniline reacts with acetic anhydride in the presence of pyridine to give acetanilide. Aniline acts as a weak base, while acetic anhydride acts as electrophile.</p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/2b7527dd-14b3-4388-8439-e7523de0f7cc-9896191_5.PNG" style="width: 503px; height: 130px;" /></p>

</details>

### Q75. JEE Main 2023 (25 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p>Match List I with List II</p><table border="1" cellpadding="1" cellspacing="1" style="width: 400px;">	<tbody>		<tr>			<td colspan="2" rowspan="1" style="text-align: center;">List I (Amines)</td>			<td colspan="2" rowspan="1" style="text-align: center;">List II <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>pKb</mi></mfenced></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>A.</td>			<td>Aniline</td>			<td>I.</td>			<td style="text-align: center;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>B.</td>			<td>Ethanamine</td>			<td>II.</td>			<td style="text-align: center;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>00</mn></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>C.</td>			<td>N-Ethylethanamine</td>			<td>III.</td>			<td style="text-align: center;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>9</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>38</mn></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>D.</td>			<td>N, N-Diethylethanamine</td>			<td>IV.</td>			<td style="text-align: center;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>29</mn></math></td>		</tr>	</tbody></table><p>Choose the correct answer from the options given below:-</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** A-I, B-IV, C-II, D-III
- **(B)** A-III, B-II, C-I, D-IV
- **(C)** A-III, B-II, C-IV, D-I
- **(D)** A-III, B-IV, C-II, D-I

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) A-III, B-IV, C-II, D-I**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Among given aliphatic amines, 2&deg; amine is most basic, followed by 3&deg; amine and 1&deg; amine.</p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d90842b5-5608-4f04-a854-440d078c7b8f-9894859_re.PNG" style="width: 782px; height: 153px;" />Therefore, the correct basic strength (K<sub>b</sub>) order of the given amines is</p><p>(C) &gt; (D) &gt; (B) &gt; (A)</p><p>The pK<sub>b</sub> order of the given amines will be just the opposite of their basic strength order.</p><p>The order is (A) &gt; (B) &gt; (D) &gt; (C)</p><p>So the correct answer is A-III, B-IV, C-II, D-I</p><p> </p>

</details>

### Q77. JEE Main 2023 (25 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The correct order in aqueous medium of basic strength in case of methyl substituted amines is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Me</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>NH</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>MeNH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>Me</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Me</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>NH</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>Me</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>MeNH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>Me</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>MeNH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>Me</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>NH</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Me</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>Me</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>NH</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>MeNH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Me</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>NH</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>MeNH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>Me</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>In aqueous medium basic strength is dependent on electron density on nitrogen as well as solvation of cation formed after accepting <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math>. In protic solvent like water, the order of basic strength is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Me</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>NH</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>MeNH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>Me</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>.</p><p>This happens beacuse in case of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Me</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>NH</mi></math> there will be optimum (+I) and solvation while in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>MeNH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> has more significant hydration than <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Me</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q78. JEE Main 2023 (24 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p>Choose the correct colour of the product for the following reaction.</p><p> </p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/32f698bc-1483-45d4-87f6-6f9ad801b5b2-9894052.png" style="width: 300px; height: 184px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** Yellow
- **(B)** White
- **(C)** Red
- **(D)** Blue

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Red**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The azo products obtained have an extended conjugate system having
both the aromatic rings joined through the &ndash;N=N&ndash; bond. These compounds
are often coloured and are used as dyes. The given diazonium salt couples with 1-naphthylamine to form a red azo-dye. This is an example of electrophilic substitution reaction.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/04b6988c-f3d9-4230-a90a-29fee18ea70c-9a0ea62e-649f-4edf-a938-cff42ef6451a-9894052_3.png" style="width: 804px; height: 220px;" /></p><p> </p>

</details>

---

### Q79. JEE Main 2023 (24 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Given below are two statements :</p><p><strong>Statement I :</strong> Pure Aniline and other arylamines are usually colourless.</p><p><strong>Statement II :</strong> Arylamines get coloured on storage due to atmospheric reduction.</p><p>In the light of the above statements, choose the most appropriate answer from the options given below :</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are incorrect
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are correct
- **(C)** Statement I is correct but Statement II is incorrect
- **(D)** Statement I is incorrect but Statement II is correct

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Statement I is correct but Statement II is incorrect**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Aniline and other arylamines are generally colourless, but they get coloured if stored outdoors due to atmospheric oxidation. Hence, statement-I is correct, and statement-II is incorrect.</p>

</details>

- **(B)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/af3218ad-2516-407f-8bd2-7db60ebb8b92-image.png" style="width: 128px; height: 110px;" /></p>

- **(D)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/6ee5bea6-f4a1-4b77-b632-b71f987feaea-image.png" style="width: 142px; height: 120px;" /></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/5c86d39d-feb7-4c78-8fbb-bdfc71136385-image.png" style="width: 181px; height: 80px;" /></p>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/54b45726-857e-4f82-b928-e7a8e4152d01-0728cccc-d1ff-48bb-9f4c-b232990445b7-image.png" style="width: 684px; height: 198px;" /></p><p>2- Naphthol aniline dye is a scarlet dye that can be prepared by coupling reaction. Aniline reacts with sodium nitrite in the presence of hydrochloric acid to form benzene diazonium chloride. Further benzene diazonium chloride reacts with 2-naphthol forms a bright orange colour 2-naphthol and forms aniline dye.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q81. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which among the following is the strongest Bronsted base?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/18ca611e-2d72-411a-a04d-e50050fef593-image.png" style="width: 146px; height: 150px;" /></p>
- **(B)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/6b31a9bb-d023-436b-85f1-33e28b267017-image.png" style="width: 101px; height: 160px;" /></p>
- **(C)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/fd4b3517-7eb2-4163-aadd-2d0405576489-image.png" style="width: 76px; height: 130px;" /></p>
- **(D)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f252c50d-408f-4d15-a87d-9c981cb02774-image.png" style="width: 77px; height: 100px;" /></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f252c50d-408f-4d15-a87d-9c981cb02774-image.png" style="width: 77px; height: 100px;" /></p>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Cyclic amines are more basic than open chain amines of same degree. Because open chain amines undergo umbrella inversion which decreases the basic nature. Hence, among the given compounds, quinuclidine is strongest base.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f252c50d-408f-4d15-a87d-9c981cb02774-image.png" style="width: 77px; height: 100px;" /></p><p>And also quinuclidine is more basic and more nucleophilic than triethylamine, because of lesser steric hindrance in quinuclidine than in triethylamine. Lesser steric hindrance in quinuclidine is due to all the alkyl groups are &ldquo;tucked&rdquo; back and Nitrogen is more exposed and can easily donate its lone pair electrons.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q82. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p>Given below are two statements : one is labelled as Assertion <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and the other is labelled as Reason <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math></p><p>Assertion <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> : Aniline on nitration yields ortho, meta &amp; para nitro derivatives of aniline.</p><p>Reason <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math> : Nitrating mixture is a strong acidic mixture.</p><p>In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math> are true and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math> is the correct explanation of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>
- **(B)** Both <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math> are true but <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math> is NOT the correct explanation of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> is true but <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math> is false
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> is false but <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math> is true

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Both <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math> are true and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math> is the correct explanation of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p> Direct nitration of aniline yields tarry oxidation products in addition to the nitro derivatives. Moreover, in the strongly acidic medium, aniline is protonated to form the anilinium ion which is meta directing. That is why besides the ortho and para derivatives, significant amount of meta derivative is also formed.</p><p> </p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/8c19a1eb-b400-4512-b6b1-6849933f10d0-image.png" style="width: 489px; height: 150px;" /></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q83. JEE Main 2022 (27 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

An organic compound &#39;<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>&#39; contains nitrogen and chlorine. It dissolves readily in water to give a solution that turns litmus red. Titration of compound &#39;<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>&#39; with standard base indicates that the molecular weight of &#39;<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>&#39; is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>131</mn><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>2</mn></math>. When a sample of &#39;<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>&#39; is treated with aq. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaOH</mi></math>, a liquid separates which contains <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math> but not <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cl</mi></math>. Treatment of the obtained liquid with nitrous acid followed by phenol gives orange precipitate. The compound &#39;<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>&#39; is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/666d1ef0-3915-407a-8868-0933c26e6901-image.png" style="width: 93px; height: 120px;" /></p>
- **(B)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/c6418ed0-10e3-4f33-9bbb-d695ef10cb69-image.png" style="width: 104px; height: 110px;" /></p>
- **(C)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/0030bd30-0e84-4d9b-80a2-1e6e32106ef0-image.png" style="width: 140px; height: 110px;" /></p>
- **(D)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/da66032f-2476-436e-8888-570b8914cfc4-image.png" style="width: 91px; height: 120px;" /></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/da66032f-2476-436e-8888-570b8914cfc4-image.png" style="width: 91px; height: 120px;" /></p>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Anilinium chloride is acidic in solution and turns blue litmus red. With Nitrous acid, it forms benzendiazonium chloride. Which reacts with phenol in alkaline medium gives orange coloured solution where the reaction follows electrophilic substitution reaction and the electrophile is benzene diazonium ion</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/0b9dae57-3b70-4e90-b66f-84ad3fbd0c90-12-08-2022neww.png" style="width: 473px; height: 303px;" /></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q84. JEE Main 2022 (26 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p>The correct sequential order of the reagents for the given reaction is</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ece47c9f-078b-4262-a18b-9234167f2378-image.png" style="width: 169px; height: 130px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>HNO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi>Fe</mi><mo>/</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>HNO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi>KI</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>/</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>HNO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi>KI</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>Fe</mi><mo>/</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>HNO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>/</mo></math> warm
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>HNO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi>KI</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>HNO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi>Fe</mi><mo>/</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>,</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>/</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>HNO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi>Fe</mi><mo>/</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>,</mo><mi>KI</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>HNO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>/</mo></math> warm

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>HNO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi>KI</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>Fe</mi><mo>/</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>HNO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>/</mo></math> warm**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The product formation takes by diazotisation of amine group followed by aromatic nucleophilic substitution reaction of iodide. Then converting nitro group into amine by reduction and again diazotisation followed by nucleophilic substitution reaction with warm water.</p><p> </p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ea2be1b1-dfb1-41af-9d5c-45b4d19e6d1c-image.png" style="width: 478px; height: 320px;" /></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q85. JEE Main 2022 (26 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Hydrolysis of which compound will give carbolic acid?

**Options:**

- **(A)** Cumene
- **(B)** Benzenediazonium chloride
- **(C)** Benzal chloride
- **(D)** Ethylene glycol ketal

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) Benzenediazonium chloride**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>When an aromatic primary amine is treated with nitrous (NaNO<sub>2 </sub>+ HCl) acid at 273 &ndash; 278 K, diazonium salts are obtained. These diazonium salts are highly reactive in nature. Upon warming with water, these diazonium salts finally hydrolyze to phenols with the liberation of nitrogen gas. Phenols can also be obtained from diazonium salts by treating it with dilute acids.<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/885ae620-3219-4cce-ad36-9462fd882dcc-image.png" style="width: 330px; height: 120px;" /></p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q86. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

An organic compound &#39;<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>&#39; on reaction with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> followed by heating gives compound <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math>. Which on further strong heating gives compound <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>8</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><msub><mi>NO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>. Compound <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> on sequential reaction with ethanolic <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KOH</mi></math>, alkyl chloride and hydrolysis with alkali gives a primary amine. The compound <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/8ae6ad9d-1514-4e73-8cdc-1cd7812860ec-image.png" style="width: 114px; height: 70px;" /></p>
- **(B)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d8b861b7-5e2b-4ead-87a8-71d908995a8c-image.png" style="width: 124px; height: 70px;" /></p>
- **(C)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/8fdd1037-c4d9-405f-8311-4b98f965572c-image.png" style="width: 126px; height: 70px;" /></p>
- **(D)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/106b6ad3-090b-4dc8-922d-3153a288e69c-image.png" style="width: 112px; height: 70px;" /></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/8fdd1037-c4d9-405f-8311-4b98f965572c-image.png" style="width: 126px; height: 70px;" /></p>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The carboxylic acids on heating with ammonia give amides. A diamide on strong heating give an imide.</p><p>Gabriel Phthalimide reaction: The hydrogen of the imide is acidic which can be extracted with base. Now, the nucleophile formed undergo bimolecular nucleophilic substitution reaction with alkyl chloride. Now, the alkyl imide undergo hydrolysis to give primary amine.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/15d5f95a-da10-4f05-af3b-24d8a8e49414-image.png" style="width: 362px; height: 400px;" /></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q87. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/15163db8-fc51-42d9-bfe9-4cf2c66e4199-image.png" style="width: 196px; height: 200px;" /></p><p>Consider the above reaction, the product <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and product <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> respectively are</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/8b3bd3a6-b5fa-4bf8-913d-167c14b08b8f-image.png" style="width: 210px; height: 120px;" /></p>
- **(B)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/51f318b9-7e38-47e9-a643-df5eca011483-image.png" style="width: 150px; height: 120px;" /></p>
- **(C)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/0a206bfc-a194-4e98-8241-86aca7e4e32e-image.png" style="width: 283px; height: 120px;" /></p>
- **(D)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/a284b626-d8b8-4745-b13f-4958282e31fd-image.png" style="width: 214px; height: 120px;" /></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/8b3bd3a6-b5fa-4bf8-913d-167c14b08b8f-image.png" style="width: 210px; height: 120px;" /></p>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>In aniline <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is a strong activating group. So, on direct bromination, substitution will occur at all ortho and para positions. And also we can not use lewis acid since it will react with lewis acid. But if we first convert this into amide then only para substitution will be major product.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/cac2fee0-4bb6-437d-b296-0e7a3b66c868-image.png" style="width: 500px; height: 300px;" /></p>

</details>

### Q88. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>With respect to the following reaction, consider the given statements:</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/b270c54f-0b75-4d88-8d16-b0c425534727-image.png" style="width: 300px; height: 79px;" /></p><p>(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></math>-Nitroaniline and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></math>-nitroaniline are the predominant products.</p><p>(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></math>-Nitroaniline and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>-nitroaniline are the predominant products.</p><p>(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>HNO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> acts as an acid.</p><p>(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> acts as an acid.</p><p>Choose the correct option.</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mfenced></math> are correct statements.
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></mfenced></math> are correct statement.
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></mfenced></math> are correct statements.
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mfenced></math> are correct statements.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></mfenced></math> are correct statements.**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Nitration of aniline gives otho, para and meta nitro anilines but para and also meta are formed in significant amounts,</p><p>meta isomer is formed due to the formation of anilinium ion,</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/63451f7e-b2ea-47f6-8d6e-89d731a1d360-image.png" style="width: 450px; height: 181px;" /></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> is strong acid, hence</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/87868b20-0d54-4047-9b13-cf839c0f96e6-image.png" style="width: 430px; height: 125px;" /></p>

</details>

### Q89. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

A primary aliphatic amine on reaction with nitrous acid in cold <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>273</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> and there after raising temperature of reaction mixture to room temperature <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>298</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>, gives a/an

**Options:**

- **(A)** Alcohol
- **(B)** nitrile
- **(C)** diazonium salt
- **(D)** secondary amine

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Alcohol**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>A primary aliphatic amine on reaction with nitrous acid in cold gives a diazonium salt, but diazonium salt of aliphatic amines are not much stable, so, they dissociate on increasing temperature slightly and gives alcohol with water.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><munderover><mo>&#8594;</mo><mrow><mn>273</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mi>HNO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced></mrow></munderover><msup><msub><mi>RN</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo></msup><munderover><mo>&#8594;</mo><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>283</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mrow></munderover><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>OH</mi><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q90. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Identify the major product formed in the following sequence of reactions :</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/e35ff29a-30e3-4268-962b-08fc2e587348-image.png" style="width: 220px; height: 115px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d5609911-8fdd-4368-bf8b-63cb4951e5e0-9709570_A.png" style="width: 130px; height: 140px;" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/a5c5c1df-3f8b-408c-aa89-db03c88dad0e-image.png" style="width: 150px; height: 196px;" />
- **(C)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d7327b03-94f4-4fd2-a43a-b0e0dd89d8dc-9709570_CV.png" style="width: 130px; height: 116px;" />
- **(D)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/72f63400-0e34-4f51-b2e1-0304f54a94b7-9709570_D.png" style="width: 80px; height: 131px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d7327b03-94f4-4fd2-a43a-b0e0dd89d8dc-9709570_CV.png" style="width: 130px; height: 116px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>In first step bromination reaction will take place, since amine group is strong activating group, bromination will take place at all ortho and para positions. Then it will give diazonium salt which on reaction with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> will give sym-tribromobenzene.</p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/8658d9f9-c0bb-4e65-89fa-011c5d531c77-ce3544cb-bf80-49e6-8f07-50ef9fb642d6-9709570_Solution.png" style="width: 450px; height: 322px;" /></p>

</details>

### Q91. JEE Main 2022 (27 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Given below are two statements :</p><p>Statement - I : In Hofmann degradation reaction, the migration of only an alkyl group takes place from carbonyl carbon of the amide to the nitrogen atom.</p><p>Statement - II : The group is migrated in Hofmann degradation reaction to electron deficient atom.</p><p>In the light of the above statements, choose the most appropriate answer from the options given below</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are correct.
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are incorrect.
- **(C)** Statement I is correct but Statement II is incorrect.
- **(D)** Statement I is incorrect but Statement II is correct.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Both Statement I and Statement II are correct.**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The mechanism of Hoffmann bromamide degradation is shown below,<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/8dde28c7-b5ed-4210-be40-5201a4b495a1-9707370.png" style="width: 600px; height: 294px;" /></p><p>In this reaction of alkyl as well as aryl group can migrate to electron deficient nitrogen atom. This migration of alkyl group is taking place towards electron dificient N-atom.</p>

</details>

### Q92. JEE Main 2022 (26 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/65112b34-4a31-40b2-9410-f5b0a3b92ccc-402c5fee-1f7d-4e32-b294-2bafdfbff36e-image.png" style="width: 340px; height: 75px;" /></p><p>Identify &#39;<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi></math>&#39; among the following</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/1eea4aa5-fd29-43e6-833e-dbe17c2adecf-9-7-20226.png" style="width: 166px; height: 124px;" />
- **(B)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/910848ad-8590-4085-84c7-a6c4c1f537f5-9-7-20227.png" style="width: 118px; height: 104px;" />
- **(C)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/6bd4d7b7-b41b-4d79-bf7e-3873f7473d18-9706381_A.png" style="width: 200px; height: 132px;" />
- **(D)** <p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/b73c2c39-1132-4c37-acf0-6165f233485d-image.png" style="width: 281px; height: 90px;" /> </p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/6bd4d7b7-b41b-4d79-bf7e-3873f7473d18-9706381_A.png" style="width: 200px; height: 132px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>First step involves the reduction of nitrobenzene to aniline in presence of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Sn</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>in</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>HCl</mi></math>. Second step is a diazotization reaction  where aniline is converted to benzenediazonium chloride in presence of nitrous acid, and third step involves the reaction between benzene diazonium chloride and beta-naphthol to form 1-phenylazo-2-naphthol an orange red dye.</p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d77ba067-6eb6-4c2c-8b04-084ed40b8214-26-06-2022.png" style="width: 450px; height: 268px;" /></p>

</details>

### Q93. JEE Main 2022 (26 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which statement is NOT correct for p-toluenesulphonyl chloride?

**Options:**

- **(A)** It is hinsberg&#39;s reagent
- **(B)** It is used to distinguish primary and secondary amines.
- **(C)** On treatment with secondary amine, it leads to a product, that is soluble in alkali.
- **(D)** It does not react with tertiary amine.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) On treatment with secondary amine, it leads to a product, that is soluble in alkali.**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>P-toluenesulphonyl chloride, which is also known as Hinsberg&#39;s reagent, It reacts with primary and secondary amines to form sulphonamides.</p><p>Tertiary amines do not react with P-toluenesulphonyl chloride.</p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/03623fc0-4cc4-43c5-aed1-327624c23673-9-7-20224.png" style="width: 460px; height: 377px;" /></p>

</details>

### Q94. JEE Main 2022 (26 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Among the following structures, which will show the most stable enamine formation?</p><p>(Where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Me</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>)</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/0f94a2c8-344a-4213-9839-59ad07896cc8-a.JPG" style="width: 200px; height: 195px;" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/c3ea74e1-1294-453c-8da5-cd4a0f1770c6-b.JPG" style="width: 200px; height: 166px;" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/220f6115-adb8-42eb-97f5-24b599327974-c.JPG" style="width: 200px; height: 189px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d82ac420-d896-456c-93af-d87ceb73a0ae-d.JPG" style="width: 200px; height: 147px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/220f6115-adb8-42eb-97f5-24b599327974-c.JPG" style="width: 200px; height: 189px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

All these enamines are interconvertible through their resonating structures. So most stable form is &lsquo;C&rsquo; due to steric factor. All of these has the equal resonance only differ in their geometrical configuration. The enamine in option C has bulky groups on opposite side of double bond. means they have the minimum repulsion between them.

</details>

### Q96. JEE Main 2022 (24 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

The conversion of propan-<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math>-ol to n-butylamine involves the sequential addition of reagents. The correct sequential order of reagents is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <p>(i) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>SOCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></p><p>(ii) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KCN</mi></math></p><p>(iii) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>/</mo><mi>Ni</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>Na</mi><mfenced><mi>Hg</mi></mfenced><mo>/</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><mi>OH</mi></math></p>
- **(B)** <p>(i) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>HCl</mi></math></p><p>(ii) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>/</mo><mi>Ni</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>Na</mi><mfenced><mi>Hg</mi></mfenced><mo>/</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><mi>OH</mi></math></p>
- **(C)** <p>(i) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>SOCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></p><p>(ii) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KCN</mi></math></p><p>(iii) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></p>
- **(D)** <p>(i) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>HCl</mi></math></p><p>(ii) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <p>(i) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>SOCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></p><p>(ii) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KCN</mi></math></p><p>(iii) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>/</mo><mi>Ni</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>Na</mi><mfenced><mi>Hg</mi></mfenced><mo>/</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><mi>OH</mi></math></p>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Propyl alcohol undergo nucleophilic substitution reaction with thionyl chloride to give propyl chloride. Propyl chloride give butane nitrile on reaction with potasium cyanide. Butane nitrile on reduction give butyl amine.<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ddb57688-e1a7-4b45-aeb4-26c994a1048d-3ae804f4-aeca-40e3-80ee-aec9125c09f9-image.png" style="width: 469px; height: 147px;" /></p>

</details>

### Q99. JEE Main 2021 (31 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/e9b07b12-df56-46d2-bb6d-061163971aba-image.png" style="width: 568px; height: 141px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/5767e9dd-588a-4514-ae42-2c2104c5bdb0-image.png" style="width: 362px; height: 181px;" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/8d8706fb-c310-44cb-bf82-68beb0518b09-image.png" style="width: 460px; height: 212px;" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/0f4d08e2-24ab-4a62-ba03-0225dbe123ef-image.png" style="width: 460px; height: 239px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/69abcfb2-5661-41cb-b931-e71cb9ea6b1b-image.png" style="width: 402px; height: 182px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>In first step, acetylation of aniline occurs. This process is done to lower the activating nature of amine otherwise poly substitution will occur.</p><p>On bromination, bromine group is directed at para position. In ortho position repulsion will dominate due to which its percentage of ortho product is less.  <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/55b5f149-1249-48f0-a57c-d958ba2114da-image.png" style="width: 901px; height: 231px;" /></p>

I. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Sn</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>HCl</mi></math>
II. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Sn</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mi>OH</mi></math>
III. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Fe</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>HCl</mi></math>
IV. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Zn</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>HCl</mi></math>
V. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mi>Pd</mi></math>
VI. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo></math> Raney Nickel

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

### Q102. JEE Main 2021 (27 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which of the following is not a correct statement for primary aliphatic amines?

**Options:**

- **(A)** Primary amines can be prepared by the Gabriel phthalimide synthesis.
- **(B)** Primary amines are less basic than the secondary amines.
- **(C)** The intermolecular association in primary amines is less than the intermolecular association in secondary amines.
- **(D)** Primary amines on treating with nitrous acid solution form corresponding alcohols except methyl amine.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) The intermolecular association in primary amines is less than the intermolecular association in secondary amines.**

**Detailed Solution:**

The intermolecular association in primary amines is more than the intermolecular association in secondary amines due to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></math> atoms are present on nitrogen.

</details>

### Q111. JEE Main 2021 (18 Mar Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

An organic compound <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> on treatment with benzene sulfonyl chloride gives compound <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math>. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> is soluble in dil. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaOH</mi></math> solution. Compound <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>NHCH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>NHCH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/3c0e9eed-d8b1-4822-8186-822bbd015bcd-image.png" style="width: 191px; height: 82px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/3c0e9eed-d8b1-4822-8186-822bbd015bcd-image.png" style="width: 191px; height: 82px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Hinsberg reagent (Benzene sulfonyl chloride) gives reaction product with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></math> amine, and it is soluble in dil. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaOH</mi></math>.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/77fd2edc-9d77-4da6-9940-2f2ba835e23a-image.png" style="width: 376px; height: 365px;" /></p>

</details>

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/93e0dee1-073d-4bc4-a33f-f3b4245147e1-image.png" style="width: 342px; height: 180px;" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/376bc662-f4c2-48f3-a3ad-badde7df0734-image.png" style="width: 419px; height: 136px;" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f9c6a62f-6dd4-4140-83ec-0dd994cb89c8-image.png" style="width: 421px; height: 123px;" />

### Q114. JEE Main 2021 (17 Mar Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Primary, secondary and tertiary amines can be separated using:

**Options:**

- **(A)** Para-Toluene sulphonyl chloride
- **(B)** Chloroform and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KOH</mi></math>
- **(C)** Benzene sulphonic acid
- **(D)** Acetyl amide

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Para-Toluene sulphonyl chloride**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Primary amines react with Para-toluene sulphonyl chloride to form a precipitate that is soluble in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaOH</mi></math>.</p><p>Secondary amines reacts with Para-toluene sulphonyl chloride to give a precipitate that is insoluble in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaOH</mi></math>.</p><p>Tertiary amines do not react with Para-toluene sulphonyl chloride.</p>

</details>

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><mi>COCl</mi><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#10230;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><msub><mi>CONHC</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>CN</mi><mover><mo>&#10230;</mo><mrow><mo>[</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>]</mo></mrow></mover><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mover><mo>&#10230;</mo><mi>HCl</mi></mover><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msup><mi>Cl</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>Cl</mi><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#10230;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>Cl</mi><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#10230;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The process of cleavage of the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></math> bond by Ammonia molecule is known as ammonolysis.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ex</mi><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mi>Cl</mi><mo>+</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#168;</mo></mover><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#10230;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></p>

</details>

### Q116. JEE Main 2021 (16 Mar Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which of the following is least basic?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mi>CO</mi></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#168;</mo></mover><msub><mi>HC</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#168;</mo></mover></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mi>CO</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#168;</mo></mover><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#168;</mo></mover><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mi>CO</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#168;</mo></mover><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>For the given compounds: </p><p>(1) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/e263173d-909f-477b-90bc-ec1bd25df1b2-image.png" style="width: 183px; height: 65px;" />; <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mo>.</mo></math> on Nitrogen is delocalised.</p><p>(2) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/073a483b-cd08-4eec-a6c0-8d8df26cf8f6-d6f62aed-92db-4e8a-acb0-2f592075a93c-image.png" style="width: 211px; height: 73px;" />; <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mo>.</mo></math> on Nitrogen is localised.</p><p>(3) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/4ddce132-6826-4d9a-a456-4695dd073efb-image.png" style="width: 211px; height: 68px;" />; </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mo>.</mo></math> on Nitrogen is delocalised due to conjugation with both <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/998c953f-e3de-420e-98ee-1511086574cb-image.png" style="width: 54px; height: 59px;" /> </p><p>(Hence least basic)</p><p>(4) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#168;</mo></mover><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>; <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mo>.</mo></math> on Nitrogen is localised.</p>

</details>

### Q117. JEE Main 2021 (16 Mar Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Ammonolysis of Alkyl halides followed by the treatment with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaOH</mi></math> solution can be used to prepare primary, secondary and tertiary amines. The purpose of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaOH</mi></math> in the reaction is.

**Options:**

- **(A)** to remove basic impurities
- **(B)** to activate <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> used in the reaction
- **(C)** to remove acidic impurities
- **(D)** to increase the reactivity of alkyl halide.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) to remove acidic impurities**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p style="margin:0cm 0cm 8pt"><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d00c8462-b030-4ceb-9928-4c084fde6f6b-mlppp.png" style="width: 413px; height: 391px;" /></p><p style="margin:0cm 0cm 8pt">So the purpose of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaOH</mi></math> in the above reactions in to remove acidic impurities.</p>

</details>

### Q118. JEE Main 2021 (16 Mar Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which of the following reaction DOES NOT involve Hoffmann Bromamide degradation ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f5fdd196-71bd-41cd-be6f-fa041a4c6c5f-b9eeaa84-25ab-43ed-9962-77d476177ab8-image.png" style="width: 450px; height: 103px;" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/5738696c-f413-4175-8fc9-6d2ce74b741d-image.png" style="width: 348px; height: 82px;" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/c156fda3-1610-4cb1-ab67-2a20e9a64508-image.png" style="width: 500px; height: 108px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/c952deb3-b033-4faa-8f03-70444cb41931-94cd932f-131e-43a1-9a5e-4ae0e44f5231-image.png" style="width: 361px; height: 87px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/c156fda3-1610-4cb1-ab67-2a20e9a64508-image.png" style="width: 500px; height: 108px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Hoffmann Bromanide degradation is conversion of amide to amine with one less no. of carbon atom. Only <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mn>1</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></msup></math>amides undergo this reaction.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/9e52d013-0e7c-4f92-a648-de56ab37fa0b-defd6aa8-5617-4acb-8c32-e0e3e05b45ad-image.png" style="width: 400px; height: 226px;" /></p><p>This reaction does not involve haffmann bromanide degradation Rest all options involve haffmann bromamide degradation during the reaction of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Br</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>NaOH</mi></math> with amide.</p><p> </p>

</details>

### Q120. JEE Main 2021 (26 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>A. Phenyl methanamine</p><p>B. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>-Dimethylaniline</p><p>C. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>-Methyl aniline</p><p>D. Benzenamine</p><p>Choose the correct order of basic nature of the above amines.</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>When hydrogen atoms of the amino group of arylamines are replaced by electron donating alkyl groups, the basic character of the resulting amine increases.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/4fabeda8-8134-41f2-86ee-000700d04295-image.png" style="width: 380px; height: 177px;" /></p><p>B.S. order, Phenyl methanamine &gt;  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>-Dimethylaniline &gt; <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>-Methyl aniline &gt; Benzenamine.</p>

</details>

### Q121. JEE Main 2021 (26 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Ceric ammonium nitrate and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CHCl</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>/</mo><mi>alc</mi></math>. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KOH</mi></math> are used for the identification of functional groups present in _ and _ respectively.

**Options:**

- **(A)** amine, phenol
- **(B)** alcohol, amine
- **(C)** amine, alcohol
- **(D)** alcohol, phenol

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) alcohol, amine**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Ceric ammonium nitrate and CHCl<sub>3</sub>/alc. KOH are used for the identification of functional groups present in Alcohol and amine respectively. </p><p>Ceric ammonium nitrate for alcohol and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CHCl</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>/</mo><mi>KOH</mi></math> is carbyl amine test for primary amines.</p><p>Alcohol give positive test with ceric ammonium nitrate and primary amines gives carbyl amine test with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CHCl</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>KOH</mi></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q122. JEE Main 2021 (26 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

An amine on reaction with benzenesulphonyl chloride produces a compound insoluble in alkaline solution. This amine can be prepared by ammonolysis of ethyl chloride. The correct structure of amine is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>NHCH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/910678ed-bc82-4386-aa0c-c55c69d87fb6-image.png" style="width: 200px; height: 65px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/b5759ba7-7834-47e3-a0c1-b49df3bcbf86-image.png" style="width: 225px; height: 82px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/910678ed-bc82-4386-aa0c-c55c69d87fb6-image.png" style="width: 200px; height: 65px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>An amine on reaction with benzenesulphonyl chloride produces a compound insoluble in alkaline solution. This amine can be prepared by ammonolysis of ethyl chloride.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/a2d89ebc-0f1a-4494-8fec-b588ff427386-image.png" style="width: 263px; height: 174px;" /></p><p>It has to be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></math> amine because on reaction with benzene sulphonylchloride it gives water in soluble product. As it is formed by ammonolysis of ethylchloride, so it has to be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>NH</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>Et</mi></math> type.</p>

</details>
