### Q123. JEE Main 2021 (24 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The total number of amines among the following which can be synthesized by Gabriel synthesis is ________.<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/bb3aa5d9-a9c3-4c90-99b0-162edf7df407-image.png" style="width: 339px; height: 125px;" /></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **3**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Gabriel phthalimide synthesis is used to prepare <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></math> aliphatic/alicyclic amine in common.</p><p>Hence, amine which can synthesised by Gabriel phthalimide synthesis method is:</p><p>(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Me</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>CH</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></p><p>(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></p><p>(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ph</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q124. JEE Main 2021 (24 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The diazonium salt of which of the following compounds will form a coloured dye on reaction with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#946;</mi></math>-Naphthol in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaOH</mi><mo>?</mo></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/e71ae45c-aac6-4fbd-9e40-5ab09d8b4675-image.png" style="width: 139px; height: 97px;" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/b69fec52-07bc-4dcc-bb13-ccd4f5855654-image.png" style="width: 113px; height: 68px;" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/0add7b76-ea95-4a08-a2b1-06a4c53f4c98-image.png" style="width: 143px; height: 70px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/cf2779df-5820-4697-9b04-16687ca35b32-image.png" style="width: 154px; height: 71px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/b69fec52-07bc-4dcc-bb13-ccd4f5855654-image.png" style="width: 113px; height: 68px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The colour obtained is the colour of the compound formed in the reaction of aniline with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NaNO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#8203;</mo><mo>/</mo><mi>HCl</mi></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mn>0</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> followed by coupling with &beta;-naphthol.</p><p>Aniline on reaction with<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>NaNO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>/</mo><mi>HCl</mi></math> forms a diazonium salt. This diazonium salt is then coupled with &beta;-naphthol.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/efee9b3d-987d-4749-9cef-dd50f873052c-image.png" style="width: 352px; height: 270px;" /></p>

</details>

### Q126. JEE Main 2020 (06 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>The increasing order of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>pK</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub></math> values of the following compounds is :</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d8bf8ae0-ec52-4dd3-99ce-8264fa9f78ac-image.png" style="width: 458px; height: 210px;" />  </p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>II</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>IV</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>III</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>II</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>IV</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>III</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>II</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>III</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>IV</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>II</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>III</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>IV</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>II</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>IV</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>III</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><msub><mi>PK</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>&#8733;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mtext>&#160;Basic strength&#160;</mtext><mfenced><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi>b</mi></msub><mo>&#8733;</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math> effect <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close=""><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></mfenced></math> density)
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>&#8733;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mtext>&#160;effect&#160;</mtext></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi>b</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>3</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>PK</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>3</mn></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/974bf0a5-75f1-40f6-984e-bcdd77e7bef0-image.png" style="width: 700px; height: 185px;" />  </p><p> </p>

</details>

### Q127. JEE Main 2020 (05 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>The increasing order of basicity of the following compounds is :</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d085f518-e567-424e-9589-c189cde8a843-image.png" style="width: 324px; height: 137px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>)</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>)</mo></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The basic nature of amines can be explained on the basis hybridisation. The basic nature order is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>&#62;</mo><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>sp</mi></math>. If the lone pair on nitrogen involved in resonance, then it is less basic than others. In the molecules A, B and D, the nitrogen is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math> hybridised. In the pyrrole molecule the lone pair on nitrogen involving in conjugation to get aromaticity. Hence, C is most basic and B is least basic.</p>

</details>

### Q128. JEE Main 2020 (04 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p>In the following reaction sequence, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>[</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>]</mo></math> is :</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/b91019a5-ea4d-407c-b39d-90e5cad6b2f1-image.png" style="width: 334px; height: 207px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/cc28ad40-542b-4d6e-8cb7-964a39e712da-image.png" style="width: 357px; height: 90px;" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/47a54704-6e6c-41cc-af11-1e46db6955f8-image.png" style="width: 353px; height: 78px;" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/051913ba-6366-4d7a-a3b2-20de427be464-image.png" style="width: 289px; height: 94px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/bb7ecb6f-35d0-4b53-9d2c-c41c14d3bda5-image.png" style="width: 302px; height: 88px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/cc28ad40-542b-4d6e-8cb7-964a39e712da-image.png" style="width: 357px; height: 90px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>To form product [C] multiple name reactions are taking place.</p><p>To form product [A] when aromatic amine react with nitrous acid it forms diazonium salt which when treated with copper salts as reagent forms aryl halides and reaction is called as Sendmeyer reaction.</p><p>Now product [A] that is when treated with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> in presence of sunlight, free radical substitution takes place and [B] is formed.</p><p>Now when [B] is treated with sodium metal in presence of dry ether forms product [B] by Wurtz reaction.</p><p>Overall reaction takes place as:</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/b8cfb47a-cf1b-4558-bc8c-7ac5fba2f330-image.png" style="width: 542px; height: 322px;" />  </p>

</details>

### Q130. JEE Main 2020 (09 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The decreasing order of basicity of the following amines is:



<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/34ec823d-877c-439b-8fff-a28f9a805af9.jpeg" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#62;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#62;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mfenced><mo>&#62;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#62;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#62;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mfenced><mo>&#62;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Basic strength depends upon availability of lone pairs. Greater the resonance of lone pairs lesser the basic strength. In <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>II</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mtext>and</mtext><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>III</mi><mo>)</mo></math> lone pair is localised. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>III</mi><mo>)</mo></math> is more basic due to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></math> effect of carbon chain whereas in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>II</mi></math>, negative mesomeric effect is operating. In <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mtext>and</mtext><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>IV</mi><mo>)</mo></math> lone pair is delocalised, so their basic strength is less that former two amines. If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>IV</mi><mo>)</mo></math> donates its lone pair then its aromaticity will get lost therefore, its basic strength is less than that of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></math>.</p><p>Overall order of basic strength is  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mi>III</mi></mfenced><mo>&#62;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mi>II</mi></mfenced><mo>&#62;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mfenced><mo>&#62;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mi>IV</mi></mfenced></math></p>

</details>

### Q131. JEE Main 2020 (09 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Consider the following reactions,

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/665e48c4-42bb-473f-ba4a-139c0a95bd5b.jpeg" style="height: 129px; width: 250px;" />
The compound <math><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/46345981-bef2-4aed-8404-d650e79da694.jpeg">
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/79c742ea-0f6f-4e00-b81b-1957aea891b1.jpeg">
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/81710246-0664-407f-975b-d60e817ee02e.jpeg">
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/2a11547b-4e7b-47f3-a3e1-e023138d3daf.jpeg">

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/81710246-0664-407f-975b-d60e817ee02e.jpeg">**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>When meta methyl aniline reacts with sodium nitrite in presence of an acidic medium then diazonium salt is formed then on reaction with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#946;</mi></math>-naphthol a colored compound is formed.</p><p>Meta methyl aniline also undergoes for the halogentaion reaction and desired compound is formed.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f4745c16-3beb-4502-a4d3-2c965b2a812d.jpeg" style="width: 400px; height: 209px;" /></p>

</details>

### Q132. JEE Main 2020 (09 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The major product <math><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi></math> obtained in the following reaction scheme is:

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/90e103b9-0349-4c2a-b5bd-299ee434bd3d-image.png" style="width: 631px; height: 169px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/7dd01d6c-47f5-42c9-8ad2-4ad335c5d8b5-image.png" style="width: 170px; height: 161px;" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/2fde4b14-6e25-4f09-a244-41c78eace4b7-image.png" style="width: 156px; height: 199px;" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/e266ff95-2982-4944-85db-96a93d7261e2-image.png" style="width: 226px; height: 174px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f1e75694-2c3c-4a97-9b40-298a4d3fabeb-image.png" style="width: 197px; height: 153px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/2fde4b14-6e25-4f09-a244-41c78eace4b7-image.png" style="width: 156px; height: 199px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/7ab15866-4983-431f-aee8-d27ea8276757-image.png" style="width: 972px; height: 225px;" /></p><p>When a diazonium salt is treated with copper (I) chloride (<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Cu</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>Cu2Cl2) or copper (I) bromide (<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Cu</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>Br</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>), the corresponding haloarene is formed. This reaction is known as Sandmeyer reaction. It is used for introducing a chloro or bromo group in the benzene ring.</p>

</details>

### Q134. JEE Main 2020 (07 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Consider the following reaction:
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/febed90a-3b33-4fe9-9a77-b4c94a079c1a.png" />
The product <math><mi mathvariant="normal">&#39;</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#39;</mi></mrow></msup></math> is used:

**Options:**

- **(A)** in protein estimation as an alternative to ninhydrin
- **(B)** in acid base titration as an indicator
- **(C)** as food grade colourant
- **(D)** in laboratory test for phenols

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) in acid base titration as an indicator**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/b1b851d8-6e0c-4f85-b64c-93b39d34b0de.png" />
Methyl orange is used as indicator in acid base titration.</p><p>Azo coupling is the most widely used industrial reaction in the production of dyes, lakes and pigments. Aromatic diazonium ions acts as electrophiles in coupling reactions with activated aromatics such as anilines or phenols.</p>

</details>

### Q135. JEE Main 2019 (12 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Benzene diazonium chloride on reaction with aniline in the presence of dilute hydrochloric acid gives:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/5721debb-c0e3-42f5-b7b9-d10a4ea18c86-image.png" style="width: 400px; height: 136px;" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/3b6c2140-03fc-4ee9-8a03-64206bbd0edd-image.png" style="width: 400px; height: 184px;" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/e0bff514-4814-4d6d-9d4b-a1d2cbf75973-image.png" style="width: 400px; height: 139px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/6d273c8a-8b2c-47d4-9096-77a39e013f2b-image.png" style="width: 400px; height: 139px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/5721debb-c0e3-42f5-b7b9-d10a4ea18c86-image.png" style="width: 400px; height: 136px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Aniline reacts with a mixture of sodium nitrite and hydrochloric acid and forms benzene diazonium chloride.The structure of Benzene diazonium chloride is</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/73f23034-1d2d-4a8d-8ccf-8106bae0d1fe2360436833253115460.png" style="width: 200px; height: 183px;" /></p><p>Benzene diazonium chloride reacts with aniline in presence of dilute hydrochloric acid and undergoes coupling reaction and produces stable azo products. In this reaction, benzene diazonium chloride acts as an electrophile in coupling with activated aniline. In this type of reaction, substitution takes place at the para position. So, the reaction Benzene diazonium chloride with aniline in presence of dilute hydrochloric acid is as follows:</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/0ec5bc6e-e016-409e-9c12-f00a40feb1b2-image5.png" style="width: 350px; height: 100px;" /></p>

</details>

### Q136. JEE Main 2019 (10 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which of the following is not a correct method of the preparation of benzyl amine from cyano benzene?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow></mrow></math>
- **(B)** (i) <math><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mrow></math> (ii) <math><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msub></math>
- **(C)** (i)  <math><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>   (ii)  <math><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msub></math>
- **(D)** (i)  <math><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msub></math>     (ii)  <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) (i) <math><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mrow></math> (ii) <math><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>When cyano benzene reacts with hydrochloric acid and water to form benzoic acid which in turn reacts with sodium borohydride to form benzyl alcohol.</p><p>Thus benzy lamine is not formed in option (b). Thus using <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>HCl</mi><mo>/</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NaBH</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> is not a correct method of the preparation of benzyl amine from cyano benzene.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f2b2558d-e6d4-4c5d-8d78-4c4ac305f4b5-image.png" style="width: 600px; height: 424px;" /></p>

</details>

### Q138. JEE Main 2019 (09 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The major products <math><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and <math><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> for the following reactions are, respectively:
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/fb45d1cd-9fc3-4f7e-ae32-efac25af8b22-image.png" style="width: 596px; height: 143px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/894ef5e4-786f-4e8f-bfdd-766755936296-image.png" style="width: 507px; height: 150px;" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/a3bd0197-9d82-46d2-b3b6-09a063e66cb3-image.png" style="width: 552px; height: 167px;" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/cfe16188-e2b9-4819-b391-aa4ffd9b8129-image.png" style="width: 544px; height: 151px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/90650fbb-e202-4e21-a877-d836e78a6b78-image.png" style="width: 508px; height: 157px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/cfe16188-e2b9-4819-b391-aa4ffd9b8129-image.png" style="width: 544px; height: 151px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

1. In polar aprotic solvent, primary alkyl halides undergo <math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>S</mtext> <mtext>N</mtext> </msub> <msup> <mrow></mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </math> reaction with <math> <mrow> <mtext>KCN,</mtext></mrow> </math> to give alkyl nitrile.

2. Alkyl nitriles, reduce in presence of <math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>H</mtext> <mtext>2</mtext> </msub> <mo>/</mo><mtext>Pd,</mtext></mrow> </math> give <math> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>1</mtext> <mtext>o</mtext> </msup> <mo>&#8722;</mo></mrow> </math> amines.

3. Cyclic ketones, on reduction with <math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>H</mtext> <mtext>2</mtext> </msub> <mo>/</mo><mtext>Pd,</mtext></mrow> </math> form <math> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>2</mtext> <mtext>o</mtext> </msup> <mo>&#8722;</mo></mrow> </math> alcohol.


<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/e8d4fe47-5190-4caa-a692-16c5551bd271-image17.png" style="width: 400px; height: 126px;" />
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ae380bb4-3187-4028-b10a-4b9af5766ac2-image18.png" style="width: 400px; height: 210px;" />
<math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></math> reduces <math><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math> into <math><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math>

</details>

### Q141. JEE Main 2019 (08 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

In the following compounds, the decreasing order of basic strength will be:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>&gt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub><mo>&gt;</mo><msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></msub><mo>)</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mo>(</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></msub><mo>)</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>&gt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub><mo>&gt;</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub><mo>&gt;</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>&gt;</mo><msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></msub><mo>)</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mo>(</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></msub><mo>)</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>&gt;</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>&gt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mo>(</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></msub><mo>)</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>&gt;</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>&gt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Basic character <math><mo>&#8733;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></math> effect
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> exert <math><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></math> effect so, more the number of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> groups more is the basic character.
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><munder><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow></msup><mtext>&#8201;Amine</mtext></mrow></munder><mo>&#60;</mo><munder><mrow><msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>)</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow></msup><mtext>&#8201;Amine</mtext></mrow></munder></math>

</details>

### Q143. JEE Main 2019 (08 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Which of the following amines can be prepared by Gabriel phthalimide reaction?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Gabriel phthalimide is used to prepare primary amine.
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f0756ad1-a414-40ae-9e0d-0e2ee87e2ca7-image.png" style="width: 500px; height: 317px;" /></p><p>In first step <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></msub><mn>2</mn></math> reaction takes place which is possible in less stearically hindered chloride. Alkyl chloride required for other amines has high stearic hindrance therefore, stearically hindered amine can&#39;t be produced from those alkyl chlorides.</p>

</details>

### Q145. JEE Main 2019 (12 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p>The increasing order of reactivity of the following compounds towards reaction with alkyl halides directly is:

<math><mo>(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>)</mo></math> <img height="144" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/7bc4f01a-8df5-4660-895a-4c5b76a295c7-image.png" width="213" />

<math><mo>(</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>i</mi><mo>)</mo><mi>&#160;</mi></math> <img height="195" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/785bc409-bb51-4ff2-b518-d98c98422a80-image.png" width="191" />

<math><mo>(</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>i</mi><mo>)</mo></math> <img height="157" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/48da1488-f142-43b3-95ed-af6e2e190ca2-image.png" width="164" />

<math><mo>(</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>v</mi><mo>)</mo></math> <img height="111" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/9b44863e-8580-4684-bcd7-063042a91717-image.png" width="163" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&lt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&lt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&lt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>v</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&lt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&lt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&lt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>v</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&lt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&lt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>v</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&lt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&lt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&lt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>v</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&lt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&lt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&lt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&lt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>v</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p style="text-align: justify;">Alkyl halide undergoes nucleophilic substitution reaction. More nucleophilic nitrogen of amine group, more reactive will it be with alkyl halides.</p><p style="text-align: justify;">Presence of electron-withdrawing group will decrease the nucleophilic character of amino group.</p><p>Hence the order of reactivity;</p><p><img height="160" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/3c8544f3-97eb-463e-884d-c8b00f21371a-image.png" width="375" /></p><p><img height="202" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/54609d56-b5ae-4c1e-92db-6f8a77889113-image.png" width="447" /></p>

</details>

### Q148. JEE Main 2019 (09 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>The increasing basicity order of the following compounds is:

<img height="37" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/66f403ee-6c9f-4486-a178-599bce7f12e1-image.png" width="175" />

<img height="35" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/26f8c59c-a976-4cea-af7e-2357fa46a7b0-image.png" width="351" />

<img height="76" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/33c047f7-a1b6-4515-b531-98c820aea0e7-image.png" width="202" />

<img height="71" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/35eaa73f-bf1e-4828-b0e9-15dd8bf76bb8-image.png" width="164" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mo>(</mo><mi>D</mi><mo>)</mo><mi> </mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi> </mi><mo>(</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>)</mo><mi> </mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi> </mi><mo>(</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>)</mo><mi> </mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi> </mi><mo>(</mo><mi>A</mi><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(B)** <math><mo>(</mo><mi>D</mi><mo>)</mo><mi> </mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi> </mi><mo>(</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>)</mo><mi> </mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi> </mi><mo>(</mo><mi>A</mi><mo>)</mo><mi> </mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi> </mi><mo>(</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi> </mi><mo>(</mo><mi>A</mi><mo>)</mo><mi> </mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi> </mi><mo>(</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>)</mo><mi> </mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi> </mi><mo>(</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>)</mo><mi> </mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi> </mi><mo>(</mo><mi>D</mi><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(D)** <math><mo>(</mo><mi>A</mi><mo>)</mo><mi> </mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi> </mi><mo>(</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>)</mo><mi> </mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi> </mi><mo>(</mo><mi>D</mi><mo>)</mo><mi> </mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi> </mi><mo>(</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>)</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mo>(</mo><mi>D</mi><mo>)</mo><mi> </mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi> </mi><mo>(</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>)</mo><mi> </mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi> </mi><mo>(</mo><mi>A</mi><mo>)</mo><mi> </mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi> </mi><mo>(</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>)</mo></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

In case of polar protic solvents, two factors decide the basic character. The positive inductive effect from alkyl groups which increases basicity.

<math><msup><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msup><mo>&#62;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msup><mo>&#62;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msup></math> Amine

&amp; the Steric Hindrance. <math><msup><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msup><mo>&#62;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>&#160;</mi></mrow></msup><mo>&#62;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>o&#160;</mi></mrow></msup></math> 

Thus, the resultant basic character is determined by taking the above two factors into account.

So order will be <math><msup><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msup><mo>&#62;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msup><mo>&#62;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msup></math> if methyl group is present as alkyl group otherwise order will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></math>.

In aromatic &amp; Aliphatic: Aliphatic Amine are more basic than aromatic because the lone pair of <math><mi>N</mi></math> is involved in delocalization.

So basic character is in the order <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mn>2</mn><mi>o</mi></msup><mo>&#62;</mo><msup><mn>1</mn><mi>o</mi></msup><mo>&#62;</mo><msup><mn>3</mn><mi>o</mi></msup><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></math>

So (B) &gt; (A) &gt; (C) &gt; (D)

</details>

### Q150. JEE Main 2018 (16 Apr Online)

### Q151. JEE Main 2018 (15 Apr Shift 2 Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The increasing order of diazotisation of the following compounds is:
(a) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ySUJfvUpb6nWeJZgzUKtQLmURpN8NPCwT9u3n5IAwak.original.fullsize.png">

(b)<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/NttUY-ohIUyYx2sscQCp4OhJQFDgDLI9PKgzMRWEoUo.original.fullsize.png">

(c)<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/gcjT3olzXq3NizbdkoqaVv42VDQ7CfVilNqW4cWb2Ko.original.fullsize.png">

(d)<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/fa1-0LHiwH-CSdb50U-OW8pQ7wPKdvXVPx6lbXC2i_s.original.fullsize.png">

**Options:**

- **(A)** (D) $ < $ (C) $ < $ (B) $ < $ (A)
- **(B)** (A) $ < $ (D) $ < $ (B) $ < $ (C)
- **(C)** (A) $ < $ (B) $ < $ (C) $ < $ (D)
- **(D)** (A) $ < $ (D) $ < $ (C) $ < $ (B)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) (A) $ < $ (D) $ < $ (B) $ < $ (C)**

**Detailed Solution:**

Aromatic diazonium salts are more stable than aliphatic diazonium salts. The higher stability of aryl diazonium salts is due to resonance. Electron donating substituents increase electron density on benzene ring. Hereby they increase the stability of diazonium salts. Electron withdrawing substituents decrease electron density on benzene ring. Hereby they decrease the stability of diazonium salts. $-\mathrm{COCH}_3$ group is electron withdrawing and hence, diazonium salts from (D) is less stable than that from (B). Although-O- $\mathrm{COCH}_3$ is electron donating substituent, but it is present in meta position. Hence, it will not have significant effect on stability. The increasing order of diazotisation is (A) $ < $ (D) $ < $ (B) $ < $ (C).

</details>

### Q152. JEE Main 2018 (08 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The increasing order of basicity of the following compounds is : 
<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/742544ce-d59a-4787-bc7c-4770497f233e-75.jpg" style="width: 203px; height: 274px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** (iv) < (ii) < (i) <(iii)
- **(B)** (i) < (ii) < (iii) < (iv)
- **(C)** (ii) < (i) < (iii) < (iv)
- **(D)** (ii) &lt; (i) &lt; (iv) &lt; (iii)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) (ii) &lt; (i) &lt; (iv) &lt; (iii)**

**Detailed Solution:**

Correct order should be :    ii  &lt;  i  &lt;  iv &lt; iii

(i) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>is</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo></math>hybridised, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></math>Amine without delocalization.

(ii) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>is</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math> hybridised.

(iii) Guanidine is a stronger base than amidine (resonance stabilised)

(iv) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></math> Amine, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>is</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math> hybridised.

</details>

### Q157. JEE Main 2015 (10 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The correct order of basicity is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img alt="" height="120" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/Q_5_opn1_april.jpg" width="295" />
- **(B)** <img alt="" height="128" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/Q_5_opn2_april.jpg" width="322" />
- **(C)** <img alt="" height="136" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/Q_5_opn3_april.jpg" width="320" />
- **(D)** <img alt="" height="131" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/Q_5_opn4_april.jpg" width="324" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <img alt="" height="136" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/Q_5_opn3_april.jpg" width="320" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></mrow><mo>&#168;</mo></mover><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi></math> (Aliphatic amines) Most basic due to +I effect of methyl group. Methoxy group is electron donating group and increase electron density by (+M) effect on <math><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> group.

<img alt="" height="171" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/image9-0f57d4f4-8cc9-4064-87b0-537158b9fc1d.jpg" width="125" />

<math><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> group withdraws electron density from N of <math><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> and decrease basic strength.

<img alt="" height="181" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/image10-064c2962-4b03-4858-9c8f-6689bcc1818c.jpg" width="99" />
 

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></mrow><mo>&#168;</mo></mover><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi></math> Most basic because NH<sub>2</sub> connected with sp<sup>3</sup> C while in i,ii&amp;iii Connected with sp<sup>2</sup> C &amp; basic strength <math> <mo>&#8733;</mo> </math> +M/+I

basic strength <math> <mo>&#8733;</mo> </math> <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mn>1</mn> <mrow> <mo>&#8722;</mo><mtext>M</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>&#8733;</mo><mfrac> <mn>1</mn> <mrow> <mo>&#8722;</mo><mtext>I</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

NO<sub>2</sub> exert -M so least basic while -OCH<sub>3</sub> exert +M so more basic.

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/EM0102096-1.JPG" style="width: 275px; height: 160px;" />

M effect is a stronger effect than the I effect.</p><p>Aliphatic amines are more basic than aromatic amines because in aliphatic amines lone pair of electrons on nitrogen is localized and easy to donate, while in aromatic amines lone pair of electrons on nitrogen atom is delocalized and participate in resonance.</p>

</details>

### Q162. JEE Main 2014 (06 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

On heating an aliphatic primary amine with chloroform and ethanolic potassium hydroxide, the organic compound formed is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** An alkanol
- **(B)** An alkanediol
- **(C)** An alkyl cyanide
- **(D)** An alkyl isocyanide

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) An alkyl isocyanide**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p style="text-align: justify;">Reaction of aliphatic primary amine with chloroform and enhanolic potassium hydroxide leads to the formation of isocyanides/ carbylamines. This reaction is called carbylamine reaction.</p><p style="text-align: justify;"><math> <mrow> <mtext>R</mtext> <mo>-</mo> <msub> <mtext>NH</mtext> <mn>2</mn>&#160; </msub> <munderover> <mo>&#8594;</mo> <mtext>KOH(alc)</mtext> <msub> <mtext>CHCl</mtext> <mn>3</mn> </msub> </munderover> <mtext>&#160;R&#160;-&#160;N</mtext> <mo>&#8801;</mo> <mtext>C</mtext> </mrow> </math>

This is the confirmatory test for primary amines. A foul smelling isocyanide is formed.

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>&#8800;</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math></p>

</details>

### Q163. JEE Main 2013 (23 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The order of basicity of amines in gaseous state is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1^{\circ}>2^{\circ}>3^{\circ}>\mathrm{NH}_3$
- **(B)** $3^{\circ}>2^{\circ}>\mathrm{NH}_3>1^{\circ}$
- **(C)** $3^{\circ}>2^{\circ}>1^{\circ}>\mathrm{NH}_3$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{NH}_3>1^{\circ}>2^{\circ}>3^{\circ}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $3^{\circ}>2^{\circ}>1^{\circ}>\mathrm{NH}_3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The correct order of relative basicity of amines in the gas phase is $3^{\circ}>2^{\circ}>1^{\circ}>\mathrm{NH}_3$ The alkyl group releases electron and thus, tends to disperse the positive charge of the alkyl ammonium ion and therefore stabilises it. Since, $\mathrm{NH}_4{ }^{+}$(from $\mathrm{NH}_3$ ) has no such alkyl group, it is not stabilised to such an extent as alkylammonium ion.

</details>

### Q165. JEE Main 2012 (12 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The conversion of benzene diazonium chloride to bromobenzene can be accomplished by

**Options:**

- **(A)** Reimer-Tiemann reaction
- **(B)** Friedel-Crafts reaction
- **(C)** Gattermann reaction
- **(D)** Azo-coupling reaction

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Gattermann reaction**

**Detailed Solution:**

Diazonium salts react with copper powder and hydrogen bromide to form bromobenzene. This reaction is known as Gattermann reaction.

### Q169. JEE Main 2007

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Which one of the following is the strongest base in aqueous solution?

**Options:**

- **(A)** Trimethylamine
- **(B)** Aniline
- **(C)** Dimethylamine
- **(D)** Methylamine

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Dimethylamine**

**Detailed Solution:**

In aqueous solution basicity order of $1^{\circ}, 2^{\circ}$ and $3^{\circ}$ amine with methyl group is 
$2^{\circ}>1^{\circ}>3^{\circ}$
In case of aniline lone pair of nitrogen is involved in resonance, so it is weaker base than aliphatic amines.
Hence, (C) is correct.

</details>

### Q173. JEE Main 2003

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The correct order of increasing basic nature for the bases $\mathrm{NH}_3, \mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{NH}_2$ and $\left(\mathrm{CH}_3\right)_2 \mathrm{NH}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\left(\mathrm{CH}_3\right)_2 \mathrm{NH} < \mathrm{NH}_3 < \mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{NH}_2$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{NH}_3 < \mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{NH}_2 < \left(\mathrm{CH}_3\right)_2 \mathrm{NH}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{NH}_2 < \left(\mathrm{CH}_3\right)_2 \mathrm{NH} < \mathrm{NH}_3$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{NH}_2 < \mathrm{NH}_3 < \left(\mathrm{CH}_3\right)_2 \mathrm{NH}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\mathrm{NH}_3 < \mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{NH}_2 < \left(\mathrm{CH}_3\right)_2 \mathrm{NH}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

More is the no . of $+\mathrm{I}$ groups attached to $\mathrm{N}$ atom greater is the basic character.

</details>
